# FM-switching — Experiment Registry

One row per experiment, in rough chronological order. Append new rows at the bottom.
Never edit a row's verdict after its result JSON is committed; add a new row instead.

**Purposes:** fidelity · cost · orchestration · feasibility · positioning · archive

**Status:** canonical · superseded · archived · pending
**Sanity-check column (from 2026-10-15): `attn_implementation` is a REQUIRED field** — record the backend actually used (asserted after load, not merely requested), and label any comparison across backends BACKEND-MISMATCHED. Jetson rows are SDPA permanently (no flash-attn/triton on aarch64).

---

## NOTICE — audit of 2026-11-03

An audit of the written record flagged rows in this registry. Flags appear in the **E-id cell**
so no column is displaced; all rows are retained and no verdict text was rewritten. Full
recomputation and evidence: **`reports/audit_2026_11_03.md`**.

- **`[INVALID]`** — the experiment does not test what it reports. Do not cite it, and do not
  recompute from its result files.
  - **E24, E24b, E24c** — the compared policies are deterministic single-point rules whose
    action spaces are disjoint singletons (`JointPolicy` = predicted node + cheapest-sufficient
    fidelity; `FidelityOnlyPolicy` = current node + cheapest; `PlacementOnlyPolicy` = predicted
    node + always full). None contains another, so "joint vs decomposed" was never compared.
    **Not evidence for or against joint optimization in either direction.**
  - **StudyI2 (SPATIAL, TEMPORAL), StudyI3 (TEMPORAL), StudyI3-reanalysis** — these arms sample
    `[float(i) for i in range(n_req)]`, i.e. the **first** N seconds of the video, not the N
    seconds before the question. Bites whenever `question_time` exceeds the cap: I2 SPATIAL
    543/559 trials (97.1%), I2 TEMPORAL 90/200 (45.0%), I3 TEMPORAL 424/918 (46.2%).
    **SPARSE is unaffected** — it samples uniformly across `[0, question_time]`.
  - **StudyD, StudyD2** — Thinking was decoded greedily (`do_sample=False, temperature=None`)
    and the repetitions are byte-identical: all 240 repetition groups in D and all 420 in D2.
    Any spread across reps is zero by construction.
  - **StudyJ (as a gate)** — temperature 0.6, `max_new_tokens=2048`, and the trial file stores
    no score or correctness field at all, so no gate could be evaluated from it.
- **`[CONFOUNDED (Thinking arms only)]`** — the Thinking arm is sampled at temperature 0.6 while
  its Instruct comparator is greedy, so decoding is not matched across the arms being compared.
  **Instruct arms are unaffected.** Verified in each script before marking.
- **`[VALID AT TEMPERATURE 0.6 ONLY]`** — StudyJ4 is a seed-variance study at temperature 0.6;
  it is valid as a statement about behaviour at that temperature and does not generalise.
- **`[SAME WINDOW DEFECT IN THE DENSE ARM]`** — StudyI's `study_i_tier_gap.py` DENSE arm carries
  the identical first-N-seconds pattern as StudyI2/I3 (37/38 DENSE trials have qt > 42). Reported
  here because the audit asked for it to be checked; the arm is small (38 trials vs 918 SPARSE).

**E36 is deliberately NOT flagged.** Its mechanism_verification cell already records
`NOT PERFORMED`, and `reports/e36_fleet_policy.md` is already marked SUPERSEDED. Where it is
cited is listed in `reports/audit_2026_11_03.md`.

**Before citing any row, check its flag.**

| StudyJ **[INVALID AS A GATE — audit 2026-11-03]** | feasibility | Reasoning gate — does Qwen3-VL-4B-Thinking beat 4B-Instruct on navigational sign recognition (SiGNgapore2D)? PRE-REGISTERED DECISION RULE: premise HOLDS only if THINKING > INSTRUCT under a paired test at α=0.05 overall; pattern of primary interest is THINKING > INSTRUCT on the highest-complexity bin. If THINKING ≤ INSTRUCT, the on-device thinking setting is NOT supported on this workload. 3 arms: INSTRUCT, THINKING, THINKING-QUICK ("answer quickly" appended). Difficulty axis = n_gt_tuples per sign (derived from ground truth). Recognition only, GT crops, no detection. Analyses A–F: accuracy per arm×bin; paired gate test; reasoning spend vs complexity + within-bin IQR/median; think-tokens vs score correlation within bins; latency-instruction effect; sanity vs paper's GPT-4o/Gemini-2.0-Flash numbers. Known confound to check: crop size may co-vary with complexity, confounding difficulty with input tokens. | `experiments/signs/study_j_signs.py` | HF NickyZimmerman/SiGNgapore2D (160 images, 205 signs); github.com/AdaCompNUS/Sign-Understanding (scoring code); arXiv 2506.02556; Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct + 4B-Thinking snapshots /mnt/ssd/, bfloat16, cuda:1; INSTRUCT: greedy, max_new_tokens=512; THINKING/THINKING-QUICK: temperature=0.6, top_p=0.95, top_k=20, min_p=0, do_sample=True, max_new_tokens=2048, 3 seeds. TOKEN BUDGET RATIONALE (pre-registered methodological change, 2026-09-29): at max_new_tokens=16384 the measured mean per-sign latency on an A6000 is ~370s, making full runs (~84h) unmeasurable. 2048 tokens (~45s/sign on A6000, several minutes on Jetson Orin) is treated as a deployment premise: signs requiring more than 2048 thinking tokens are truncated and score as failures. The gate is therefore testing "does thinking beat instruct under a deployable token budget," not unlimited compute. Truncation rate and non-termination rate are primary results, not caveats. | StudyJ2 **[CONFOUNDED (Thinking arms only) — audit 2026-11-03]** | feasibility | Survival curve of reasoning length on SiGNgapore2D — does Qwen3-VL-4B-Thinking reasoning terminate within any tested budget, or does non-termination persist? Question: is truncation a budget artifact removable by a larger budget, or does a substantial fraction of signs never terminate at any tested budget? Design: 40-sign stratified subset (13 low/13 mid/13 high/1 other, seed=42 manifest written before run); max_new_tokens=24576 (model max_position_embeddings=262K, input ~800 tok, total ~25K well within limit); same decoding as StudyJ (temp=0.6, top_p=0.95, top_k=20, min_p=0, do_sample=True); 1 seed; skip_special_tokens=False for accurate think_closed detection. PRE-REGISTERED ANALYSES: (A) survival curve at B∈{1024,2048,4096,8192,16384,24576} by bin; (B) think-token distribution for completers, uncensored, per bin; (C) never-terminate rate per bin; (D) verdict: budget artifact (give B at <10% truncation) OR asymptote (give rate at 24576). (E) late-trace excerpt classification (class a/b) for any never-terminating trace at 24576. SCORER VALIDATION (Task 1, completed before run): Gemini-2.0-Flash reproduced at overall_acc=40.7% vs paper 42.2% (−1.5pp, within run-to-run variation). GPT-4o not in cloned repo; noted. SUBJECTS: the 8 cap-hit signs from StudyJ2 §4 + 4 control completers sampled from StudyJ2 manifest (seed=42: IMG_6599/n_gt=8, IMG_6413/n_gt=4, IMG_6538/n_gt=1, IMG_6451/n_gt=2). RUNS: 12 signs × 3 arms = 36 total. Runtime projection after 4 runs; stop if >12h projected. PRE-REGISTERED DECISION RULE: failure is INPUT-DETERMINED if per-sign failure rates are bimodal (signs cluster near 0% or near 100%); failure is STOCHASTIC if rates cluster near a common rate across signs. SUBJECTS: 8 cap-hit signs from StudyJ2 §4 + 6 control completers from StudyJ2 manifest (seed=99: 2 low, 2 mid, 2 high — IMG_0049/n_gt=1, IMG_0064/n_gt=1, IMG_6567/n_gt=3, IMG_0042/n_gt=5, IMG_6415/n_gt=7, IMG_6584/n_gt=18). Runtime projection after 5 fresh runs; if >14h, reduce to 3 seeds (42,123,456) and continue. ANALYSES: (1) per-sign failure rate out of 5 seeds for all 14 signs, sorted; (2) distribution shape against pre-registered rule; (3) any control completers failing on any seed; (4) within-sign think-token variance (min, max, max/min) for signs terminating on ≥2 seeds; (5) pooled per-attempt failure rate with binomial CI vs StudyJ2 20% (CI 9–35%); (6) last-500-token classification for signs failing all 5 seeds. | StudyJ6 **[CONFOUNDED (Thinking arms only) — audit 2026-11-03]** | feasibility | Accuracy cost of the simplified prompt — does SIMPLIFIED (StudyJ5) hurt recognition accuracy relative to ORIGINAL on the same 14 signs × 5 seeds? StudyJ5 showed SIMPLIFIED significantly reduces non-termination (McNemar p=0.0043, ORIG 25.7% → SIMP 5.7%) but could not score accuracy because `answer_text` was not stored. J6 reruns both arms from scratch on the same 14 signs × 5 seeds, storing `answer_text`, then scores with the validated open_clip scorer (ViT-B-32, laion2b_s34b_b79k, loc=soft, sym=soft-clip). SCORER VALIDATION (pre-condition): reproduce Gemini-2.0-Flash overall_acc ≈ 40.7% (J2 reference) before scoring any model trials; halt if validation fails. PRE-REGISTERED DECISION RULE: FIX if SIMPLIFIED accuracy on terminating trials is not significantly worse than ORIGINAL accuracy on the same (sign,seed) pairs (paired McNemar or Fisher on terminating subset, α=0.05); TRADE if accuracy drops significantly. ANALYSES: (A) termination rate per arm (check vs J5: ORIG ≈ 25.7%, SIMP ≈ 5.7%); (B) accuracy on terminating trials — per-arm overall_acc + deployment-realistic accuracy (terminating trials get score; non-terminating trials score 0 — penalises the arm that terminates less); (C) FIX vs TRADE verdict — paired test on (sign,seed) pairs where both arms terminate; (D) think-token distribution among completers per arm (median, IQR, min, max). Both arms use identical decoding to J5: Qwen3-VL-4B-Thinking, max_new_tokens=24576, temp=0.6, top_p=0.95, top_k=20, min_p=0, do_sample=True, skip_special_tokens=False. | `experiments/signs/study_j6_score.py` | same 14 signs as J5 (8 cap-hit + 6 control); Qwen3-VL-4B-Thinking snapshot /mnt/ssd/; bfloat16; cuda:1; open_clip ViT-B-32/laion2b_s34b_b79k on CPU; Gemini results at /tmp/sign-understanding/recognition_results/gemini/recogntion_results.json | `results/signs/study_j6/study_j6_trials_fa2.jsonl`; `reports/study_j6_simplified_accuracy_fa2.md` (flash-attn run, 2026-10-16). | StudyM | feasibility | Reasoning gate on real CPS sensor time-series — does Qwen3-8B thinking mode beat no-think on industrial sensor fault analysis (SenTSR-Bench) and physical-sensor TSRBench tasks? Prior repo gates (StudyK ERQA, StudyJ SiGNgapore2D) found no thinking gain on visual/embodied tasks. CURRENT SCOPE: smoke test only (30 SenTSR series, T-OFF and T-ON arms, 3 seeds = 540 trials). Full-scale plan (11,370 trials, 4 arms, SenTSR + TSRBench) is DEFERRED, not cancelled — see reports/study_m_plan.md §8. SMOKE TEST PRE-REGISTERED DECISION RULE: primary metric = item-level accuracy (mean over 3 seeds), paired T-ON minus T-OFF on 60 reasoning items (How Happened + Suggested Fix). Paired bootstrap 95% CI (10,000 resamples) + Wilcoxon signed-rank p-value. GO: difference ≥ 10 pp AND CI excludes 0 AND T-OFF reasoning accuracy ≥ 35%. NO-GO: difference < 5 pp OR CI upper bound < 10 pp OR T-OFF reasoning accuracy ≤ 30%. INCONCLUSIVE: anything else (next step = scale to all 110 series). The full-scale 5 pp BH-adjusted rule is retained for the deferred full study and does NOT apply to the smoke test. DATASETS: SenTSR-Bench (ZLHe0/SenTSR-Bench, CC-BY-NC-4.0): 30 of 110 series selected with numpy.random.default_rng(seed=42).choice(110,30), indices [7,8,12,17,18,36,37,43,45,47,48,49,50,54,59,61,63,68,71,73,75,82,83,84,86,88,89,94,96,105]. ARMS: T-OFF (Qwen3-8B, enable_thinking=False, temp=0.7, top_p=0.8, top_k=20, min_p=0, max_new_tokens=1024), T-ON (Qwen3-8B, enable_thinking=True, temp=0.6, top_p=0.95, top_k=20, min_p=0, max_new_tokens=16384). Decoding from Qwen3-8B model card verbatim. Vision arms, TSRBench, and ECG deferred. Qwen3-8B snapshot: b968826d9c46dd6066d109eabc6255188de91218. ALSO REPORT (descriptive): A1. Flip structure (majority vote): right/right, wrong/wrong, helped, hurt cells + McNemar on discordant pairs; same table per seed to show seed noise. Reasoning items and perception items separately. A2. Seed noise floor: within-arm correctness churn fraction per arm; compare to cross-arm flip rate. A3. Variance decomposition of T-ON think tokens: log(think_tokens) ~ stage + (1|series) + (1|item), residual = seed; variance shares by stage/series/item/seed; repeated on helped items only; budget-hit traces treated as censored. A4. Think-token distribution in helped items: median, IQR, range; Wilcoxon vs. both-correct items (descriptive, not gate). What Happened paired comparison; per-stage accuracy with 95% Wilson CIs; T-ON think-token distributions (median, IQR, p90, max) per stage; budget-hit rate; non-termination rate; wall time and tok/s. STOP: if projected runtime >12h before starting. See reports/study_m_plan.md §8 for full descriptive spec. | `experiments/ts/study_m_smoke.py` (to be written after commit) | SenTSR-Bench (CC-BY-NC-4.0, ZLHe0/SenTSR-Bench); Qwen3-8B snap b968826d9c46dd6066d109eabc6255188de91218 (/mnt/ssd/hf_models/models--Qwen--Qwen3-8B/); A6000 cuda:1 | `results/ts/study_m_smoke/study_m_smoke_trials.jsonl`; `reports/study_m_smoke_results.md` | a6000 (cuda:1) | 30 series × 3 stages × 2 arms × 3 seeds = 540 trials | PENDING — awaiting commit then script authoring | pending | N/A (gate study) | N/A (gate study) |
| StudyM-toff | feasibility | StudyM smoke test — T-OFF arm only. Context: the pre-registered 540-trial T-OFF+T-ON smoke test hit the 12h runtime stop condition at trial 10 (mean latency 87.5s; T-ON trials 66–341s; projected 12.9h > 12h). Decision per user 2026-10-01: run T-OFF arm only; do not raise threshold; run T-ON only if floor clause does not fire. DESIGN: same 30 SenTSR-Bench series, 3 stages, 3 seeds; T-OFF arm only (Qwen3-8B enable_thinking=False, temp=0.7, top_p=0.8, top_k=20, min_p=0, max_new_tokens=1024, snap b968826d9c46dd6066d109eabc6255188de91218). 270 trials. FLOOR CLAUSE (pre-registered): NO-GO if T-OFF reasoning accuracy (item-level, How+Fix) ≤ 30%. RESULT: T-OFF reasoning accuracy 27.2% (Wilson CI 17.6%–39.6%), which is ≤ 30%. FLOOR CLAUSE FIRES → NO-GO. Per-stage: what=17.8%, how=20.0%, fix=34.4%. All-stage 24.1%. Letter bias: A=37.8%, B=10.0%, C=21.5%, D=30.7%. Seed agreement 88.9% (80/90 items unanimous). 270/270 parse=ok. VERDICT: NO-GO — T-ON not run. T-OFF cannot constitute a meaningful comparison baseline; both arms would likely perform near chance. See reports/study_m_smoke_toff.md. IMPLEMENTATION ISSUES FOUND: (1) initial T-OFF trials had answer_text→think_content routing bug (parse_failed all T-OFF), fixed; (2) <|im_end|> in stored answer_text (cosmetic, parse unaffected), fixed. | `experiments/ts/study_m_smoke.py` | SenTSR-Bench (CC-BY-NC-4.0, ZLHe0/SenTSR-Bench) 30 series; Qwen3-8B snap b968826d9c46dd6066d109eabc6255188de91218; A6000 cuda:1 | `results/ts/study_m_smoke/study_m_smoke_trials.jsonl` (274 total: 270 T-OFF + 4 T-ON from aborted run); `reports/study_m_smoke_toff.md` | a6000 (cuda:1) | 270 T-OFF trials (30 series × 3 stages × 3 seeds) | NO-GO — floor clause fires (T-OFF reasoning 27.2% ≤ 30%) | complete | N/A (gate study — floor clause fires before gate tested) | N/A (gate study) |
| StudyN | feasibility | Is evidence horizon predictable at runtime from question text and elapsed time? S-EMBER grounding annotations (n=9,448; 10 excluded for negative nearest distance; 9,438 used). For each QA: nearest evidence distance = question_time − answer_end_time; farthest = question_time − answer_start_time. Sanity check target: nearest p50/p75/p90 = 21/56/117 s; farthest 74/135/236 s (from H2). INCLUDE counting_objects_events; report with and without. Targets: T1 = log(farthest+1), T2 = binary evidence within W ∈ {60,120,300} s. Predictors (runtime-available only; category label ORACLE-only): P0 constant, P1 elapsed time, P2 keyword rules (first/earlier/before/at the beginning/last time/just/now/currently), P3 TF-IDF+ridge/logistic+elapsed time, P4 all-MiniLM-L6-v2+logistic+elapsed time, P5 ORACLE-INFO (category+elapsed time). Cross-validation: 5-fold grouped by video_id, seed=42. Metrics: T1 Spearman+MAE; T2 AUROC+calibration (10 bins). Systems metric (primary): coverage (fraction questions whose full evidence lies in retained window) for Policy A (fixed trailing W) vs Policy B (per-question choice between W_s and W_l matched to mean W budget; oracle is budget-constrained). Gap closed = (B−A)/(oracle−A). PRE-REGISTERED DECISION RULE (written before results computed): PREDICTABLE if best non-oracle predictor closes ≥40% of oracle coverage gap at W=120 with 95% bootstrap CI (resampling videos) excluding 0; UNPREDICTABLE if <15% or CI includes 0; PARTIAL otherwise. Per-category breakdown of coverage gap. 20 random questions with true distance and predictor predictions. RESULT: Best predictor P4 (all-MiniLM-L6-v2): T1 Spearman ρ=0.445, T2 AUROC=0.738 at W=120. Systems metric: Policy A=70.3%, Oracle (binary)=75.4%, P4 Policy B=60.2%, gap_closed=−196.1% (95% CI: −254.9%–−153.6%, includes 0). The negative is specific to the tested policy family — binary W_s=W/2 and W_l=2W at matched mean budget. Under that family, even the binary oracle gains only 5.1 pp over the fixed window. COMPUTED LIMITATION: an exact (continuous) oracle that assigns each question its farthest distance cheapest first at mean budget W gives coverage 84.3%/100.0%/100.0% at W=60/120/300; at W=120 the binary W_s/W_l discretization costs 24.6 pp of headroom before any predictor error. Predictive signal exists (ρ=0.445, AUROC=0.738) but does not translate to coverage gain under this policy family. Per-query window adaptation is not realizable in streaming systems; N2 tests escalation instead. VERDICT: UNPREDICTABLE — under binary W/2–2W policy family; escalation framing not yet tested. | experiments/sember/study_n_predict.py | results/sember/study_h/data/sember_grounding.jsonl (n=9,448); all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (sentence-transformers); CPU | results/sember/study_n/study_n_results.json; reports/study_n_results.md | flash (CPU) | 9,438 QA pairs × 6 predictors × 5 folds | UNPREDICTABLE — under binary W/2–2W policy family; predictive signal present (P4 ρ=0.445, AUROC=0.738) but not sufficient for coverage gain. | complete | PASS | N/A (predictive study) |
| StudyN2 | feasibility | Escalation framing: with device holding last L seconds and remote tier holding full history, can runtime-available information (question text + elapsed time) choose which queries to escalate better than random escalation at the same escalation rate? DISCLOSURE: an exploratory analysis on the same data using TF-IDF+log(elapsed) at L=120, e=0.2 found gap_closed ≈47% (AUROC 0.79). N2 is a confirmatory re-implementation with a pre-registered rule, not independent evidence. DATA: S-EMBER grounding annotations (same exclusions as StudyN, n=9,438; also report without counting_objects_events). Grouped 5-fold CV by video_id, seed=42. Features: question text and question_time only. SETTINGS: local window L ∈ {60,120}; escalation rate e ∈ {0.1,0.2,0.3}. TARGET: needs_escalation = (farthest > L). POLICY: escalate top e fraction by predicted P(needs_escalation). Covered if (not escalated and farthest ≤ L) or (escalated, full history assumed). BASELINES: random at rate e (analytic: (1-p)+e*p where p=frac needing escalation); oracle (escalate true positives first). PREDICTORS: P1 elapsed-only; P2 keyword rules (same as StudyN); P3 TF-IDF(1-2gram,2000,sublinear)+log(1+qt), logistic; P4 all-MiniLM-L6-v2+log(1+qt), logistic; P5 ORACLE-INFO category+log(1+qt). METRICS: AUROC per (L,predictor); coverage and gap_closed per (L,e,predictor); 95% CI bootstrap over videos (2000 resamples, resample test-fold predictions, no refit); lookup-table check (best text predictor by category); calibration (10 bins) for best predictor at L=120. SANITY CHECKS: p(needs_escalation) at L=60≈0.579, L=120≈0.297. PRE-REGISTERED DECISION RULE (written before computing): primary cell L=120, e=0.2, best of P1-P4 by training-fold AUROC. SIGNAL: gap_closed ≥40%, CI lower bound >15%. NO SIGNAL: gap_closed <15% or CI includes 0. PARTIAL: otherwise. Report also whether text predictors beat P1 elapsed-only (paired bootstrap on primary cell). | experiments/sember/study_n2_escalation.py | results/sember/study_h/data/sember_grounding.jsonl (n=9,438); all-MiniLM-L6-v2; CPU | results/sember/study_n2/study_n2_results.json; reports/study_n2_results.md | flash (CPU) | 9,438 QA pairs × 5 predictors × 5 folds × 2 L × 3 e | SIGNAL — P4 (MiniLM) selected by OOF AUROC=0.766; gap_closed=44.9% CI=[41.9%,48.0%] (video-clustered bootstrap, n=3141 videos), lower bound 41.9%>15% and value ≥40%. Text predictors beat P1 elapsed-only: Δgap=21.7% CI=[18.3%,25.0%]. cov_pred=82.5% vs random=76.2% vs oracle=90.3%. DISCLOSURE: exploratory prior found ≈47%; this run confirms at 44.9%. MODEL-SELECTION DEVIATION: pre-registration said "training folds only"; implementation used mean OOF AUROC (standard CV selection). In-fold AUROC selects P3 (0.937 train) instead of P4 (0.765 OOF test); P3's in-fold 0.937 is scaler-induced overfitting (see below) — P4 is the correct selection by OOF. P3 AUROC DISCREPANCY (implementation fix found after results were read): main script applies StandardScaler(with_mean=False) to sparse TF-IDF matrix; unit-variance scaling inflates rare n-grams causing overfitting (in-fold 0.937 vs test 0.703). External run used no scaler → test 0.789 matching ref. Without scaler: P3 OOF=0.789, in-fold=0.859. P3 no-scaler gap_closed at primary cell = 48.1%; P3 no-scaler OOF (0.789) exceeds P4 OOF (0.766) so P3 no-scaler would have been selected, but gap_closed 48.1% ≥ 40% → verdict SIGNAL unchanged. P2 scaler Δ=0.000, P4 scaler Δ=+0.015 (both dense; scaler is standard for dense features). Primary result unchanged. | complete | PASS | N/A (predictive study) |
| StudyO **[CONFOUNDED (4B-T x RICH cell only) — audit 2026-11-03]** | orchestration | Corrected S-EMBER state × compute factorial — does the gain from a larger model or thinking mode depend on whether retained frames contain the evidence? Fixes audited I2/I3 bug: frames must be from [0, question_time], not the first N seconds. Three frame-selection arms: RECENT (trailing W at 1fps), UNIFORM (16 frames over [0, qt]), RICH (256 frames over [0, qt], pixel budget binding). Three model arms: 4B-I (Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct), 8B-I (Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct), 4B-T (Qwen3-VL-4B-Thinking). Q1 (primary): mixed-effects logistic correct ~ model_arm × evidence_covered + state_arm + (1\|question_id). Q2 (secondary): best state arm per (category,distance bin). Q3 (secondary): counting accuracy by count bin. Q4 (secondary): ESCALATION POLICY EVALUATION — does N2's P4 predictor (retrained excluding Study O videos) convert coverage gain to end-to-end accuracy gain? Local = 4B-I×RECENT(120s); remote = 8B-I×RICH (and 4B-T×RICH on thinking subset). Policies at e∈{0.1,0.2,0.3}: (a) never escalate, (b) always escalate, (c) random at rate e, (d) predictor top-e, (e) oracle (farthest distance), (f) accuracy oracle (escalate where remote correct and local wrong, up to rate e). Metric: end-to-end accuracy from factorial per-question outcomes (no extra inference if local and remote are factorial cells). Primary Q4 test: acc(d)−acc(c) at e=0.2, paired bootstrap clustered by video; also report fraction of (c)-(f) gap closed. SAMPLE CHECK: confirm ≥29% of sample has farthest>120s; compute min detectable acc(d)−acc(c) at 80% power. Kill conditions K1 (Q1 interaction <3pp n.s.), K2 (per-category lookup ≥80% oracle gap), K3 (all Instruct arms within 5pp chance), K4 (acc(d)−acc(c) <1pp at e=0.2 with CI including 0 → N2 signal stays at coverage level only). Plan: reports/study_o_plan.md. No inference this task. | experiments/sember/study_o_*.py (to be written) | S-EMBER (facebook/S-EMBER); Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct, 8B-Instruct, 4B-Thinking; all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (for Q4 predictor); A6000 cuda:1 | results/sember/study_o/ (after approval) | a6000 (cuda:1) | TBD (stratified sample; power calc in plan §8) | HALTED AT THE §8 SAMPLE CHECK 2026-10-14 — NO INFERENCE RUN. Report: `reports/study_o_results.md`. All FOUR defects flagged 2026-10-03 were found UNFIXED and have now been fixed in the plan: D1 the 4B-T arm still named Qwen/Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct "(thinking mode)" — corrected to Qwen/Qwen3-VL-4B-Thinking with all three snapshots resolved and verified distinct (4B-I ebb281ec, 8B-I 0c351dd0, 4B-T 1de27d8c); without this the study would have run the 4B-I checkpoint twice and reported one as a thinking arm. D2 decoding was unspecified — frozen to CFG-A (thinking temp=0.6/top_p=0.95/top_k=20/min_p=0, instruct greedy) with the model card's temp=1.0 deviation disclosed. D3 max_new_tokens unspecified — set 8192 with think_closed/budget_hit required per trial. D4 evidence_covered was coded 1 for RICH and UNIFORM "since they span [0,qt]" — redefined as "does any SAMPLED frame timestamp fall within [answer_start_time, answer_end_time]" and recomputed from actual sampled timestamps over the 520 locally-available questions: RECENT 68.8%→90.4% (+21.5pp), UNIFORM 100%→85.4% (−14.6pp), RICH 100%→99.6% (−0.4pp). THE CORRECTION INVERTS THE ARM ORDERING — under the old coding RECENT was the only uncovered arm so evidence_covered was nearly collinear with "is this RECENT", meaning Q1's interaction would have re-estimated the arm main effect under another name; under the corrected coding UNIFORM is the WORST-covered arm (85.4%), below RECENT (90.4%). Mechanism: UNIFORM inter-frame gap median 15.9s (max 77s) vs evidence-window median 29s (p25 10s), and 31.5% of questions have an evidence window narrower than their own sampling gap. HALT CAUSE: §8.1 Q4 power PASSES (31.2% farthest>120s vs ~29.7% expected) but §8.2 Q1 cell balance FAILS — 12 of 24 (category × distance_bin) cells hold <20 questions. NOT repairable by resampling: only 150 of the 3,141 referenced videos are present locally (results/sember/study_i/videos, 19GB), restricting StudyO to 520 of 9,448 questions; full balance needs ~480 evenly spread and the 520 available are uneven, so the check fails even using the entire local pool. Root cause is data availability (~400GB of videos absent), not sample size. OPEN FOR DECISION: the halt is triggered by the Q1 mixed-effects requirement; Q4 — the question that matters for the paper — does not depend on cell balance and its own §8.1 check passed, so a Q4-only run on the 520 local questions using 3 of the 9 cells (4B-I×RECENT, 8B-I×RICH, 4B-T×RICH) may be viable. Q4-ONLY RUN AUTHORISED AND COMPLETED 2026-10-15 — report `reports/study_o_q4_results.md`, trials `results/sember/study_o/study_o_q4_trials.jsonl` (1560 = 3 cells × 520, 4.83h GPU, within the 8h cap, no sample reduction needed). AUTHORISATION RATIONALE: Q1 tests the evidence × capability interaction and the H100 StudyR run is closing that independently — 32B-Thinking vs 32B-Instruct b=6, c=6 at n=53, the same net-zero reshuffling seen at 4B (ERQA 59/49) and 8B (MV-RoboBench 71/67); if capability heterogeneity does not exist at any runnable scale Q1 has nothing to find, whereas Q4 asks whether StudyN2's escalation predictor (OOF AUROC 0.766, 44.9% of oracle coverage gap closed) converts to end-to-end ACCURACY and is the live thread for the paper. **VERDICT: K4 FIRES — coverage-based escalation does NOT convert to accuracy; the N2 runtime-signal claim stays at the coverage level only.** PRIMARY (remote = 8B-I×RICH, paired n=520): local 4B-I×RECENT 33.85% [29.9,38.0], remote 33.27% [29.4,37.4] — REMOTE IS 0.58pp WORSE; discordance b=58 (11.2%) c=61 (11.7%) net −0.58pp; acc(d predictor top-e) − acc(c random) at e=0.2 = **+0.50pp, 95% CI [−1.18,+2.27]** (paired bootstrap 2000 resamples clustered by video_id, 150 clusters), CI includes 0; gap_closed_accuracy 4.4% against an accuracy-oracle headroom of +11.27pp. K4 reason (i): local and remote too similar, model routing does not matter. SECOND REMOTE (4B-T×RICH, plan §7 requires both): 30.00% [26.2,34.1], 3.85pp WORSE than local; b=58 c=78 net −3.85pp; d−c=+1.54pp CI [−0.32,+3.59] — K4 does NOT fire there ONLY because the rule is a conjunction ((d)−(c)<1pp AND CI includes 0) and 1.54>1pp; the CI still includes 0 so the result is null, and the larger apparent gain is partly mechanical — a worse remote drags the random baseline (c) down, making it easier to beat; never-escalate (33.85%) beats always-escalate (30.00%) by 3.85pp. DEEPER FINDING (the paper-relevant one): the EVIDENCE-DISTANCE ORACLE — policy (e), ranking by the TRUE farthest-evidence distance, i.e. perfect knowledge of the quantity N2's predictor estimates — gains only +1.85pp (8B-I) and +0.57pp (4B-T) at e=0.2, against accuracy-oracle headroom of +11.27/+11.92pp. Real exploitable headroom exists (~11-12pp) but a PERFECT coverage signal reaches only 5-16% of it, so coverage-based escalation is limited by the signal being near-ORTHOGONAL to which-arm-is-correct, NOT by predictor quality; improving the predictor cannot fix this. N2's SIGNAL verdict is confined, not contradicted. P4 retrained on 8928 rows with all 520 StudyO questions on 150 videos excluded, no-leakage asserted in code; base rate 0.296; P(farthest>120s)=31.2% vs H2 expectation ~29.7%. D4 coverage FROM SAMPLED TIMESTAMPS: RECENT 90.4% (matches precheck exactly), RICH 100%. STATED DEVIATION: RICH implemented as 1fps over [0,qt] capped at 256 by even subsampling (= StudyI3's validated TEMPORAL arm) rather than the plan's literal "256 frames", so 56% of RICH trials carry <256 frames (median 240; median 146 for the qt<256s half); sub-second sampling would need a finer decode than the 1fps cache and would diverge from prior S-EMBER work; D4 is unaffected since coverage uses actual sampled timestamps; token economics confirm the match at 55 tok/frame inside StudyI3's committed 44-56 range. IMPLEMENTATION DEFECT CAUGHT PRE-RUN: a first version passed frames as individual images, bypassing the processor pixel cap at 80,229 input tokens/question (663 tok/frame, 36s latency); caught by cross-checking StudyI3's committed ~11,264-token figure, corrected to the video path with VideoMetadata and max_pixels=1280*28*28 → 12,451 tokens, 2.4s; the 10 affected trials were discarded. SANITY: 4B-T think_closed 516/520 (99.2%), budget_hit 4/520 (0.8%), think tokens median 188 IQR[101,406] max 4922 so the 8192 budget was not binding; parse failures reported separately — 4B-I 0, 8B-I 0, 4B-T 4, and all 4 coincide exactly with the 4 budget hits, scoring them all correct gives 30.77% and leaves the verdict unchanged; all arms well above the 20% chance baseline so this is a real-mechanism null, NOT a degenerate one (contrast StudyS1's 137-block where both arms sat at chance). COST: local 2.4s, 8B-I remote 3.5s (1.5×), 4B-T remote 13.2s (5.5×) for −3.85pp. ATTENTION BACKEND (recorded 2026-10-15): study_o_q4.py passes no attn_implementation so all three cells ran on the transformers 5.12.1 default, SDPA (verified: flash_attn 2.6.3 is installed on flash but not requested; a no-arg load reports _attn_implementation=sdpa for text and vision). The committed S-EMBER cost studies DO request flash_attention_2 (study_i2_budget.py:457, study_i3_budget.py:557), so Q4's ABSOLUTE latencies are not comparable to those curves and must not enter cost_matrix.csv without a backend-matched rerun; the within-study ratios (1.5x/5.5x) and ALL accuracy results including the K4 verdict are unaffected, since the backend is shared across the three cells and changes attention numerics rather than the selected option letter. This is a consistency-check-4 miss (one named quantity, two configurations) caught 2026-10-15, not by the checklist. STILL NOT RUN: Q1, Q2, Q3 — the 9-cell factorial remains halted on §8 cell balance, repairable only by ~400GB of absent video. | Q4 complete; Q1/Q2/Q3 still halted | PASS — chance baseline marked, arms above chance, parse failures separated, scoring sensitivity bounded, no-leakage asserted, bootstrap clustered by video | PASS — mechanism present and exercised: all three arms well above the 20% chance baseline with 23% genuine discordance, so the null is a real negative rather than a degenerate one; the policy ladder additionally isolates WHY (coverage is near-orthogonal to which-arm-is-right, shown by the evidence-distance oracle reaching only 5-16% of the accuracy-oracle headroom). |

ARMS: A = 4B-Thinking 1 pass (seed 42); B = 4B-Thinking up to 5 passes, retry only when trace fails to terminate, seeds 42,123,456,789,1011 in order (arm B reuses arm A as pass 1); C = 8B-Thinking 1 pass (seed 42); D = 8B-Instruct 1 pass greedy (bounded baseline). Audit 1: the planned direct/compositional contrast DOES NOT EXIST — it is determined by category (6/8 categories 100% uniform on structural markers), so the pre-registered interaction is a between-category comparison, not an orthogonal factor. Within-category homogeneity is favourable for Gate 2: spatial_viewpoint_identification has 256 questions but only 2 distinct question strings (largest byte-identical block = 137, n_views=6 sd=0); pooled IDEAL+USABLE blocks n=466, within which any static predictor has AUROC=0.5 by construction. Audit 2: CFG-A 41.0% [31.9,50.8], CFG-B 40.0%, CFG-C 40.0% — indistinguishable at n=100 (CIs span ~19pp); 0/100 budget hits in all configs, think_closed=100%; CFG-B≡CFG-C is a tautology (neither cap binds); ~6pp gap vs published 47.3% is UNEXPLAINED and not a budget artifact (nothing truncated; max think 3841 of 8192), reproducing the StudyK (−2.2pp) and StudyQ (−4.1pp) shortfall; transformers 5.12.1 so the 5.17.x loop regression is ruled out; looping question IDs differ across configs (CFG-A vs CFG-B zero overlap); cross-workload contrast 0% non-termination on ERQA vs 20–30% on SiGNgapore2D (StudyJ2) same model/decoding. FROZEN CONFIG: CFG-A on cross-study-consistency grounds, NOT accuracy; discloses deviation from the model card's temperature=1.0 recommendation. Audit 3: net-rescue n 5pp→848 / 10pp→212 / 15pp→94; MDI 3.5pp at full dataset, 9.1–10.3pp per category; interaction 424/stratum at 10pp, MDI 7.0pp at half-dataset strata; Gate 2 pooled-block n=466 MDI 6.7pp. GO/NO-GO: criterion 1 FAILS as written (strata do not exist), criterion 2 CONDITIONAL (think_closed 100% pass; 6.3pp from 47.3% marginal fail of a threshold presuming the vendor number is reproducible — it was not in StudyK or StudyQ), criterion 3 PASS. RESTRUCTURE APPROVED 2026-10-05: Gate 2 within-block test promoted to PRIMARY; direct/compositional interaction demoted to a clearly-labelled between-category comparison. Gate 2 design extended (plan v3) to THREE static-predictor variants because S1's 0.5 AUROC is structural, not empirical — within a byte-identical block the images are the only varying input and are available at arrival, so a text-only baseline is a strawman: S1 = text+metadata (MiniLM), S2 = image-only (CLIP/SigLIP mean-pooled + visual stats + query-vs-option similarity margin), S3 = S1+S2. Decision rule: Gate 2 FIRES if S2 or S3 reaches stratified within-block OOF AUROC >= 0.70 with CI lower > 0.5; SURVIVED only if all three are near chance (CI upper < 0.70) AND an oracle shows within-block net_rescue > 5pp — S1 alone is inadmissible for a SURVIVED verdict. AUROC must be block-stratified (only within-block pairs contribute) or between-block category signal inflates it. Audit 3b (Hanley-McNeil): pooled blocks n=466/69pos rule out 0.70 at observed <=0.620; the 137-block alone needs <=0.560 and is UNDERPOWERED for SURVIVED, so pooled n=466 is primary and the 137-block is confirmatory only. Criterion-2 fragility recorded: the 5pp threshold presumed the vendor 47.3% reproduces, but it never has (StudyK -2.2pp, StudyQ -4.1pp, here -6.3pp), so the p_disc=0.270 prior behind every n/MDI in Audit 3 is unverified and must be recomputed from the main experiment's own contingency table. LICENSE RISK: MV-RoboBench declares no license on HF; paper (arXiv 2510.19400, "Seeing Across Views", corresponding author Zhiyuan Feng = HF AaronFengZY24) says only "open resource". audit1_mvrobobench.json contains full question text for all 1708 questions and must be reduced to derived statistics before any public release. Full 1708-question arm T NOT RUN. Arm I 24.9% overall vs 20% chance; gold uniform, 0 extraction failures. Per-category arm I: spatial_distance_judgement 41.3%, robotic_trajectory_selection 33.5%, spatial_cross_view_matching 26.5%, robotic_step_execution 24.8%, spatial_viewpoint_identification 24.2%, robotic_action_planning 20.6%, robotic_affordance_recognition 19.6%, spatial_3d_spatial_consistency 9.3% (~3.8SD BELOW chance; model over-predicts C against uniform gold, consistent with adversarial hard negatives, not a harness fault). ARM T PILOT (n=60 on the 137-block, the Gate 2 primary instrument): arm I 23.3%, arm T 26.7% +-11.2 (CI includes chance 20%), b(I wrong,T right)=10, c(I right,T wrong)=8, discordance 30%, net_rescue +3.3pp CI [-10.5,+17.2], think_closed 60/60, budget_hit 0. INTERPRETATION: both arms at chance on the primary block with near-symmetric discordance, so the (I wrong, T correct) label there is coin-flip noise. This is the degenerate-link stopping condition — a predictor would be fitting randomness, every S-variant would land near chance, and the plan would read SURVIVED for the wrong reason. Even the FULL 137-block has MDI 12.4pp for net_rescue against an observed +3.3pp, so completing it buys an uninterpretable null. STRUCTURAL PROBLEM: instrument quality and model competence are in disjoint cells — the only block with image-valued options and the strong S2 query-to-option margin feature (137-block) is where the model is at chance, while the categories where the model is competent (distance_judgement 41.3% n=201, trajectory_selection 33.5% n=200) have largest blocks of 55 and 4 and text options, so S2 degrades to generic visual statistics. No cell has both a usable block and a non-degenerate label. RUNTIME: 44.1s/question median (3x the 14.9s ERQA-based estimate; MV-RoboBench supplies 3-6 images vs ERQA 1-2) => full arm T 20.9h, competent-categories-only (401 q) 4.9h. DECISION TAKEN 2026-10-05: option (1) approved — arm T on the competent-category stratum only (~4.9h). PRE-REGISTERED BEFORE THE RUN (plan v5, `reports/study_s0_plan.md` §StudyS1 AMENDMENT). WHY THIS IS AN INSTRUMENT REPLACEMENT AND NOT POST-HOC FISHING: the 137-block was abandoned because it CANNOT MEASURE, not because it measured against us — it was pre-registered as primary on the grounds that its static features are provably degenerate (AUROC 0.5 by construction), and the pilot showed it is ALSO label-degenerate (both arms at chance, discordance 30% near-symmetric, net_rescue +3.3pp CI [-10.5,+17.2]); a label produced by two coin flips cannot be predicted by anything, so no predictor result from it would carry information. Abandoning an instrument that cannot measure differs from abandoning one that measured unfavourably, and only the latter is fishing. The stratum was selected from ARM I ALONE, BEFORE arm T ran on it, on a criterion independent of the hypothesis — arm I Wilson 95% CI lower bound > 25% (clearly above the 20% chance baseline) — which involves no thinking-vs-instruct contrast and no quantity entering the Gate 1 test; it selects spatial_distance_judgement (41.3%, CI [34.7,48.2], n=201) and robotic_trajectory_selection (33.5%, CI [27.3,40.3], n=200), total n=401, separating cleanly from the next candidate at CI lower bound 20.9%. CONDITION 1 (honest labelling, binding): the 137-block WAS the pre-registered primary instrument; it returned a DEGENERATE LABEL; the competent-category run is a SECONDARY, POST-HOC STRATUM; the verdict must read "Gate 1 tested on a post-hoc stratum" and must never be phrased as if the original design had been executed. CONDITION 2: this run answers GATE 1 ONLY — Gate 2 required the byte-identical block, which is dead, and the competent categories have TEXT options so the query-to-option CLIP margin is undefined and only generic S2 features exist; a positive Gate 1 here does NOT advance the dynamicity claim, it establishes only that a rescue population exists and that MV-RoboBench provides no instrument to test its predictability; NO Gate 2 verdict may be reported from this run. CONDITION 3 (stop, pre-registered): Gate 1 PASSES iff net_rescue on the competent categories exceeds 5pp with a paired 95% CI excluding 0; otherwise "Neither — branch is empty", STOP — no third stratum, no further category selection, no additional workload, and a near miss does NOT license re-aiming again. Arm I is REUSED for these 401 questions (already in study_s1_trials.jsonl from the completed sweep), not re-run. Output: `reports/study_s1_competent_categories.md`. RESULT (stratum complete, n=401, 2026-10-06): **NEITHER — BRANCH IS EMPTY. STOP.** net_rescue = +1.00pp, 95% CI [-4.74,+6.74]; net>5pp NO, CI excludes 0 NO; McNemar chi2(cc)=0.065 vs crit 3.841. arm I 37.4% [32.8,42.2], arm T 38.4% [33.8,43.3], chance 20%. b(I wrong,T right)=71 (17.7%), c(I right,T wrong)=67 (16.7%), discordance 138 (34.4%) — thinking changes >1/3 of answers and nets nothing; reporting b alone would have read as a strong positive, which is exactly what Correction 1 was introduced to prevent. Per-cluster nets straddle zero and oppose: distance_judgement +2.5pp [-5.5,+10.5] (n=201), trajectory_selection -0.5pp [-8.8,+7.8] (n=200); only 2 clusters so per-cluster nets reported instead of an unreliable 2-cluster sandwich CI. CLASSIFICATION: **REAL NEGATIVE, a settled finding** (CLAUDE.md Rule 2) — distinct from the 137-block pilot which was INCONCLUSIVE/design-failure: there both arms were at chance (23.3%/26.7%) so the label was coin-flip noise, here both arms are competent (37.4%/38.4%), discordance is genuine, and thinking was exercised throughout (think_closed 95.5%, median 1689 think tokens); the mechanism was present and did not produce the claimed effect. POWER CAVEAT STATED: MDI ~6.5pp at n=401 is just above the 5pp threshold, so a true 5-6.5pp effect could have been missed; observed +1.00pp sits far below that window so the conclusion does not rest on the marginal zone. COST: arm T 43.5s/q median vs arm I 0.19s = **226x latency for +1.00pp**; 7.93h A6000 for 401 questions vs ~80s for arm I; consistent with StudyQ (8B-I matched 4B-T at 91x speed). Think tokens median 1689 IQR[873,3155] max 8117. NON-TERMINATION WORKLOAD-DEPENDENCE now 4 cells: ERQA 0%, MVRB/trajectory_selection 0%, MVRB/distance_judgement 8.5%, SiGNgapore2D 20-30% — two categories of the SAME dataset differ by 8.5pp under identical model/decoding/budget. SANITY: arm I reused 401/401 matched by qid_path; think_closed 383/401; budget_hit 18/401 (4.5%, all in distance_judgement); parse failures arm T 12 (3.0%) vs arm I 0, reported separately not silently scored wrong; 2 truncated traces emitted correct letters (scoring them correct moves acc 38.4->38.9%, net +1.00->+1.50pp, verdict unchanged); SENSITIVITY BOUND — even scoring ALL 12 parse failures correct gives acc 41.4%, net +3.99pp CI [-1.83,+9.81], still below threshold with CI including 0, so the verdict is robust to the most favourable scoring assumption available. GATE 2 NOT TESTED AND NOT REPORTED (Condition 2): the only instrument with provably degenerate static features was also label-degenerate, and these two categories have TEXT options so the query-to-option CLIP margin is undefined — MV-RoboBench provides no instrument to test rescue-population predictability. Pre-registered stop applies: no third stratum, no further category selection, no additional workload. | complete | pending | REAL NEGATIVE — mechanism present and exercised (both arms competent at 37.4%/38.4% vs 20% chance, discordance 34.4% genuine, think_closed 95.5%, median 1689 think tokens) and did not produce the claimed effect. Contrast with the 137-block pilot, where the mechanism was ABSENT (both arms at chance, label was coin-flip noise) and the result was correctly classified INCONCLUSIVE rather than negative. TWO-PART RECORD: (a) on the PRE-REGISTERED PRIMARY INSTRUMENT (137-block) the Step 2 degenerate-link check FAILED — the link "a genuine rescue population exists within the block that a predictor could learn" was ABSENT (net_rescue +3.3pp, both arms at chance, discordance symmetric); per CLAUDE.md this is a stopping condition and the full 1708-question sweep was NOT run; (b) on the POST-HOC competent-category stratum the mechanism WAS present and exercised, so the null there is a real negative rather than an artifact. |

Cap-hit labels do not transfer across stacks (HF→vLLM). The 3 'CAP_HITTERS' each hit the cap under only one HF backend. (4) Run-to-run variance: k=1 repeat (C4 equal-priority vs C3) 16 vs 6 evictions in hold, 989 vs 1,326 s normal stall → k=1 vs k=2 NOT distinguishable; all C configurations n=1. (5) All Thinking decoding at temperature 0.6 (model card 1.0; shipped generation_config 0.8): natural lengths and cap rates are setting-dependent, forced results are not. | StudyX2a | cost | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-10-17, before any run.** Does natural (unforced) non-termination exist for Qwen3-VL-32B-Thinking at the recommended sampling settings? Follows the StudyX audit: StudyX's 'stuck' leads were forced, and all StudyX Thinking decoding used temp 0.6. ARMS (every sampling parameter sent explicitly; no min_tokens, no ignore_eos): T1.0 = model card 'Generation Hyperparameters / VL' verbatim: greedy='false', top_p=0.95, top_k=20, repetition_penalty=1.0, presence_penalty=0.0, temperature=1.0, out_seq_length=40960; T0.8 = shipped generation_config.json (rev 7edd10ff): do_sample true, top_k 20, top_p 0.95, repetition_penalty 1.0, temperature 0.8 (no presence_penalty key → 0.0); T0.6 = StudyX CFG-A (temp 0.6, top_p 0.95, top_k 20, min_p 0), for continuity. DEVIATION from the card: max_tokens 24,576, not out_seq_length 40,960 (the server's max_model_len 32,768 cannot hold ~1k prompt + 40,960 anyway). A 'cap hit' therefore means 'did not close </think> within 24,576 tokens', not non-termination at the card's budget. WORKLOAD: the 23-sign StudyX normal pool × 5 seeds (42 + 1000·s, s = 0..4; the StudyX normal-stream seeds, so T0.6 repeats StudyX attempts) per arm = 115 requests per arm, 345 total. Closed loop at concurrency 12, seed-major order, arms run T1.0 → T0.8 → T0.6. Full reasoning and answer text stored. METRICS per arm: (1) cap-hit rate = completion_tokens ≥ 24,576 AND no </think>, with 95% Wilson CI; requests that closed </think> but hit the cap in the answer are counted separately. (2) Per-sign rates and overdispersion: χ² homogeneity across 23 signs, p-value by 10,000-permutation Monte Carlo; not testable if fewer than 2 cap hits. (3) Leave-one-seed-out predictability: each (sign, seed) is scored by the sign's cap-hit rate on its other 4 seeds; AUROC, and Brier score vs a leave-one-out base rate; not estimable with fewer than 2 cap hits. (4) Loop vs progress: 8-gram repetition rate (fraction of 8-token windows already seen earlier in the trace; model tokenizer) over the last 4,096 tokens of each capped trace, with terminating traces as reference; ≥ 0.5 is labelled 'loop' (descriptive threshold). (5) Median, p90 and p95 natural length of terminating traces (p95 feeds 2b's budget B). DECISION RULE (fixed before running): if the T1.0 cap-hit rate is < 2% AND its Wilson upper bound is < 5%, natural non-termination is negligible at the card setting → report plainly and STOP; 2b is not started. At n = 115 this holds only for 0 or 1 cap hits (Wilson upper 3.2% / 4.8%; 2 hits → 6.1%). RUNTIME: estimated 2.2–2.7 h, assuming every arm has StudyX T0.6 natural-normal lengths (mean 5,858 tok, median 4,326, 8% capped; 345 × 5,858 ≈ 2.0M tokens, KV-bound at about 9–10 resident requests at roughly 30–37 tok/s each). It would be about 4.6 h if the overall cap rate were about 17%. GUARD: after each arm, if elapsed + (arms left × longest arm so far) > 3 h, stop before the next arm and report. | StudyO-q4-845 | orchestration | Q4 RE-RUN ON THE ENLARGED 845-QUESTION POOL UNDER flash_attention_2. NOT a replication of StudyO's committed Q4 (reports/study_o_q4_results.md, 520 questions, SDPA): BOTH the question pool and the attention backend changed, so no number here is a before/after measurement against that row and the 520 result stands unmodified in the record. POOL CHANGE CAUSE: the committed Study O halted at the §8.2 cell-balance gate because 12 of 24 (category x distance_bin) cells held <30 questions, caused by absent video, not by sample size. A greedy set cover over the S-EMBER manifest identified the minimum repair — 84 videos, 9.97GB — fetched 2026-10-16 (experiments/sember/study_o_fetch_videos.py). | StudyR5 | cost | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-10-17, before any R5 GPU run.** Cloud fleet with warm per-device history: what does an escalation cost when the cloud keeps each device's history warm (prefix cache) vs cold, and how many devices' histories fit before warm state is evicted? Systems construction; accuracy NOT measured. SERVER: Qwen3-VL-32B-I @0cfaf481, StudyX config (vLLM 0.31.0, FA3, CUDA graphs, gmu 0.90, max_model_len 32768) with prefix caching ON and --enable-prompt-tokens-details (per-request cached_tokens), images ≤ 640 per prompt, image processor bounds 4,096–20,480 px so frames are not rescaled, localhost-bound. KV capacity (tokens) is recorded from the startup log. HISTORY: frames at 1 fps from session start, each a separate image in time order, FIXED 160×128 (H×W; 128×160 for landscape) = 5×4 = 20 visual + 2 delimiter tokens = 22 tok/frame → 10 min ≈ 13.2k tokens (the nearest 32-px grid to the 12.5k target; a probe verifies it before the sweep). A per-cell / device / session text header leads every prompt, so no prefixes are shared across devices or cells. DEVICES D ∈ {1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32}: distinct S-EMBER sessions from the 845-question pool (234 videos shuffled with seed 0, dealt round-robin; each device chains its own videos). A session runs to its last question. **REPLAY 1× (REAL TIME), CHANGED from the 10× proposed to the user earlier.** At 10×, P-WARM's appends at D=32 are about 32 requests/s, each resending up to 600 images, so API-side image preprocessing rather than KV would bound the system. Each device starts at a seeded uniform offset into its first session; questions arrive at their question_time. POLICIES: P-COLD = no uploads, each escalation sends header + all frames so far + question; P-WARM = an append every 10 s of session time (all frames so far, max_tokens 1) plus escalations. Escalation = greedy, max_tokens 4. Every question escalates (upper bound), plus 20%-escalation runs (random, seed 0) at D = 8 and 32 for BOTH policies. WINDOW: 15 min wall after warm-up (P-WARM warm-up = upload each device's history up to its offset; not measured); requests submitted in the window are measured, then in-flight requests drain. One repetition per cell, plus a second P-WARM D=8 repetition (seed 1) for run-to-run variance. FP8 KV (--kv-cache-dtype fp8), if this vLLM supports it: P-WARM at D = 8, 16, 32, with capacity and hit-rate change reported. METRICS per (policy, D): escalation TTFT and total latency (median / p90 / p99 / max); escalation token hit rate = Σcached / Σprompt over in-window escalations, plus server prefix_cache hits/queries; preemptions; KV usage; append compute = Σ(prompt − cached) tokens of appends, as a share of all computed prompt tokens and in seconds of append latency; throughput (escalations/min, computed prompt tok/s); D* = the smallest D where P-WARM's escalation hit rate < 50%. MECHANISM CHECK (before the sweep continues): at D=1, P-WARM escalations must show prefix-cache hits and lower TTFT than P-COLD; if P-WARM shows no hits at D=1, STOP. At the largest D, confirm hits drop. | StudyRH2 | cost | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-10-18, before any GPU run (user's "H2"; E-id RH2).** Latency surface near R5's knee with PARTIAL warm state: what does an escalation cost for warm vs cold devices when only some devices' histories are kept warm, at D around the bf16 knee? Systems construction; accuracy NOT measured. Everything not listed is StudyR5's, reused from `study_r5_fleet` (32B-I @0cfaf481, vLLM 0.31.0, prefix caching ON, **bf16 KV**, --enable-prompt-tokens-details, ≤ 640 images per prompt, 160×128 frames = 22 tok/frame, per-cell/device/session header; device plans = 234 videos shuffled with Random(0) and dealt round-robin over D, offsets from the same Random(0), so the same assignment construction as R5 for each D; 1× real-time replay; append every 10 s = header + all frames so far, max_tokens 1; escalation greedy, max_tokens 4; 15-min window, in-window requests measured, then drain). GRID: D ∈ {8, 12, 16, 24} × escalation rate ∈ {1.0, 0.2} × policy ∈ {P-COLD (no appends), P-WARM (all devices warm-up + append), **P-HALF** (even-index devices warm-up + append; odd-index devices never append and escalate cold)}, one repetition each, + a second repetition of the three policies at D=12, rate 1.0. Rate 0.2 = for each device, a random 20% (round(0.2 n)) of all questions in its plan, Random(0) device by device. It depends only on D, so the subset is the same across policies. **rep2 uses the same seed** (assignment, offsets) = pure run-to-run variance (R5's rep2 used seed 1). CALIBRATION (before the grid): isolated requests on 4 sessions (cold 300-s upload, +10-s append, escalation) and a fit latency = a + b·computed tokens. Per-append GPU time is not observable under batching, so **append GPU time = Σ over appends of (a + b·computed tokens)** (isolated-cost estimate), reported with the summed contended append latency. METRICS per cell, for P-HALF separately for warm and cold devices: escalation TTFT and total latency (median/p90/p99/max), per-request cached tokens and hit rate (+ token-weighted), preemptions, KV usage, append GPU time and append computed tokens, computed prompt tok/s, in flight at window end, drain time. **MECHANISM CHECK (first grid cell, P-HALF D=8 rate 1.0; STOP if it fails):** warm devices' escalation token hit ≥ 0.80, and cold devices show NO append-derived hits. Literal "no cache hits" cannot hold for cold devices: under prefix caching a cold device's escalation can reuse its OWN previous escalation's prefix (R5 caveat: COLD D8 hit 0.53–0.61). The check is therefore every cold-device escalation's cached_tokens ≤ the prompt_tokens of that device's previous escalation in the same session, and ≤ 32 tokens on a session's first (≤ two blocks of shared chat template + cell header, no frames). RUNTIME ESTIMATE (cells × (warm-up + 15 min + drain), R5 drains; WARM interpolated in D, HALF at D as R5 WARM at D/2, rate 0.2 = rate 1.0 drain): **7.6 h for all 27 cells < 9 h → nothing dropped** (drop order if over: D=24 rate 0.2, then rep2). Pre-stated expectations, not decision rules: the bf16 knee should sit between D=12 and D=16 for P-WARM, and P-HALF at D should behave like P-WARM at D/2 for its warm devices if KV residency is what drives the knee. Known in advance: the 640-image limit rejects late requests of sessions > 640 s (R5: ≈45 HTTP 400 per D32 cell); these are counted as errors. | `experiments/cost/study_rh2_knee_surface.py` (vLLM venv), `study_rh2_analyse.py` | StudyO 845-question pool's 234 S-EMBER videos (1 fps frame cache, outside the repo) | `results/cost/study_rh2/study_rh2_{requests,metrics}_h100nvl.jsonl.gz`, `study_rh2_{run_meta,results}_h100nvl.json`; `reports/study_rh2_knee_surface.md` | h100nvl GPU 3 (exclusive, session watchdog); vLLM 0.31.0+cu129 venv | 27 cells × 15 min | Run 2026-10-18 17:16 → 2026-10-19 02:09, GPU 3; KV 65,280 tok. MECHANISM CHECK PASSED (HALF D8: warm hit 0.95; cold 0/36 violations; first-in-session cached 16 tok). Cold devices next to warm ones are 0.06–0.73 s slower than pure P-COLD (D24 2.00 vs 1.41 s) and lose own-prefix hits from D16 on. Escalation rate barely moves the warm side (appends = 90–99% of computed tokens). Pre-stated expectations both held. rep2 (D12, same seed): medians within 0.03 s. IMPLEMENTATION FIX mid-run (idle-wait after window end; no request changed; ~1.6 h idle lost; one cut cell rerun). | StudyO-q4-sep | orchestration | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-10-17 BEFORE RUNNING — the separating cell.** StudyO Q4's design confounds model size with frame selection: local is 4B-I x RECENT and both remotes are x RICH, so every reported gain and every difference-in-differences is a MODEL+STATE effect and no cell isolates either. This runs the missing cell, **4B-I x RICH**, on the same 845 questions, identical to the existing RICH arm in every other respect (same frame selection and RICH_CAP=256, same ~12.6k pixel budget, same prompt, greedy decoding, max_new_tokens=512, flash_attention_2 asserted per trial). **DISCLOSURE ON WHAT "STATE" MEANS HERE:** at the matched token budget RICH carries ~1.9x the frames of RECENT at ~0.53x the tokens per frame (measured from the trials: 121 frames @ 103.0 tok/frame vs 231 frames @ 54.8 tok/frame). So the state contrast is **wider temporal coverage at lower per-frame detail under a matched budget**, NOT "more information" — it is a reallocation, and a null could mean the two effects cancel. **PRE-REGISTERED CONTRASTS**, all DiDs split by the local arm's evidence_covered flag, all CIs 2,000 video-clustered bootstrap resamples at seed 0: (1) STATE EFFECT AT FIXED MODEL = (4B-I x RICH) - (4B-I x RECENT); (2) MODEL EFFECT AT FIXED STATE = (8B-I x RICH) - (4B-I x RICH); (3) overall accuracy of all four cells with Wilson 95% CIs; (4) ESCALATION RE-RUN — the full Q4 policy grid (e in {0.1,0.2,0.3}; never / always / random / P4 / evidence oracle / accuracy oracle) with **4B-I x RICH as the remote**, to see how much escalation benefit survives WITHOUT the larger model, reporting evidence-oracle-vs-random and P4-vs-random with video-clustered CIs. **PRE-REGISTERED DECISION RULES, fixed before seeing any result:** STATE CARRIES IT = contrast 1 CI excludes 0 AND contrast 2 CI includes 0; MODEL CARRIES IT = the reverse; BOTH = both CIs exclude 0; NEITHER = both include 0, and the MDE must be stated alongside so the null is classifiable under CLAUDE.md Rule 2. **RUNTIME ESTIMATE (per the research/HOSTS.md rule):** 0.65 h for 845 trials at ~2.78 s/trial, assuming a **median generation length of 2 tokens** (greedy instruct, answer letter only), extrapolated from THIS study's own 4B-I x RECENT (2.29 s/trial) and 8B-I x RICH (3.27 s/trial) cells in study_o_q4_trials_fa2.jsonl. The estimate is HARD-BOUNDED ABOVE at 0.77 h by the measured 8B-I x RICH cell, since 4B-I x RICH runs the same frames and budget on half the parameters. Unlike the StudyO-q4-845 runtime miss, there is no generation-length tail to undersample here: both arms terminate at a median of 2 generated tokens, so the mean/median latency ratio is ~1.0 rather than 1.8. | experiments/sember/study_o_q4.py --cell 4B-I --state RICH --fa2 (new --state flag; writes its own JSONL so no existing cell's rows are mixed in); analysis in study_o_q4_analyze.py | S-EMBER 845 questions / 234 videos; Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct; all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (P4) | results/sember/study_o/study_o_q4_trials_4bi_rich_fa2.jsonl | a6000 (cuda:1) | 845 | **RESULT — PRE-REGISTERED DECISION: STATE CARRIES IT.** 845 trials, 0 errors, 0.95h, flash_attention_2 on 845/845 rows. CONTRAST 1 (state at fixed model, (4B-I x RICH)-(4B-I x RECENT) split by evidence_covered): uncovered n=97 20.6%->32.0% = +11.3pp; covered n=748 34.4%->30.3% = -4.0pp; **DiD +15.4pp, 95% CI [+3.8,+27.8], EXCLUDES 0** (SE 6.17pp, MDE 17.30pp). CONTRAST 2 (model at fixed state, (8B-I x RICH)-(4B-I x RICH)): uncovered +8.2pp, covered +5.3pp; **DiD +2.9pp, CI [-7.1,+13.4], INCLUDES 0** (SE 5.19pp, MDE 14.54pp). Rule as pre-registered: C1 excludes 0 AND C2 includes 0 -> STATE CARRIES IT. **BOTH CONJUNCTS CARRY POWER CAVEATS THAT MUST BE STATED WITH THE VERDICT.** C2's null is **INCONCLUSIVE (underpowered) under Rule 2, NOT a real negative**: its MDE 14.54pp EXCEEDS its CI upper bound +13.4pp, so a model-driven interaction nearly as large as the state one would go undetected — the defensible claim is "state is SUFFICIENT to produce the interaction", not "model contributes nothing". C1 is also under 80% power for the effect it found (MDE 17.30pp > observed 15.4pp), so +15.4pp is positive-but-poorly-determined and subject to winner's-curse inflation. **THE STATE EFFECT IS A CROSSOVER, NOT A GAIN:** 4B-I x RICH is WORSE on average than 4B-I x RECENT (30.53% [27.52,33.72] vs 32.78% [29.70,36.02], Wilson) — widening temporal coverage at the cost of per-frame detail helps +11.3pp where the evidence is OUTSIDE the recent window and hurts -4.0pp where it is INSIDE, and the 7.7:1 covered:uncovered split makes the average negative while the interaction is strongly positive. Contrast 2's pattern (+8.2pp and +5.3pp, similar in both strata) is the signature of a MAIN EFFECT: the larger model is better roughly independently of coverage, adding a level shift on top of the state-driven interaction. CONTRAST 3 (Wilson 95% CIs, all four cells): 4B-I x RECENT 277/845 32.78% [29.70,36.02]; 4B-I x RICH 258/845 30.53% [27.52,33.72]; 8B-I x RICH 306/845 36.21% [33.04,39.51]; 4B-T x RICH 261/845 30.89% [27.86,34.08] — all four intervals overlap heavily. **CONTRAST 4 IS THE HEADLINE: THE ESCALATION BENEFIT DOES NOT REQUIRE A LARGER MODEL.** With remote = 4B-I x RICH (same model on both sides, only the state changes), the evidence-distance oracle beats random escalation at EVERY e with the CI excluding zero: e=0.1 +1.05pp [+0.20,+2.11]; e=0.2 **+2.22pp [+0.88,+3.61]**; e=0.3 +2.92pp [+1.32,+4.34] — against +2.39pp [+0.92,+3.86] at e=0.2 when the remote arm was the LARGER 8B-I x RICH. Essentially ALL of the coverage signal's value comes from switching STATE REPRESENTATION, not from switching MODEL, which is a materially cheaper deployment story. P4 vs random fails at every e (+0.70 [-0.32,+1.57]; +0.45 [-0.81,+1.82]; +0.44 [-0.90,+1.91]), replicating the Part 1 underpowered-predictor finding rather than showing coverage is uninformative — the evidence oracle on this same data is significant at all three e. Accuracy-oracle headroom here is only +6.96pp at e=0.2, BELOW the 14.6% run-to-run flip rate, so "% of oracle gap closed" against that column is uninformative. **RUNTIME ESTIMATE MISSED (recorded per the HOSTS.md rule):** pre-registered 0.65h with a claimed HARD upper bound of 0.77h; ACTUAL 0.95h (4.05s/trial), 1.46x over and the bound VIOLATED. The bound assumed 4B-I x RICH must be cheaper than 8B-I x RICH at equal frames because it has half the parameters; that is wrong, because a large share of per-trial cost at 231 frames is VIDEO DECODE AND VISION-TOWER work that is identical across the two and does not shrink with LLM parameter count, and the 8B-I reference was measured with the RICH frames already warm in page cache after 4B-T traversed the same videos. New corollary for the HOSTS rule: do not extrapolate a latency across MODEL SIZES without decomposing it into components that scale with parameters and components that do not. The 2h gate was never at risk. NOT SEPARABLE HERE: frame count vs per-frame resolution — RICH changes both at once (1.9x frames at 0.53x tok/frame); a matched-frames/differing-resolution cell would separate them and was not run. Report: reports/study_o_q4_separating_cell.md. | complete | PASS — pre-registered contrasts and decision rules fixed in this row before the run; all CIs 2,000 video-clustered bootstrap resamples at seed 0; MDE reported alongside every CI so both conjuncts are classifiable under Rule 2; Wilson CIs on all four cells; runtime estimate vs actual recorded with the cause of the miss. | PASS — mechanism present and exercised, and for the first time ISOLATED: the 2x2 corner that separates model size from frame selection was run, so the interaction is attributable to state rather than to model+state jointly. Negative control is built in: contrast 4 holds the model FIXED and the evidence-oracle effect survives at every e with CI excluding zero, demonstrating the effect does not depend on the model change. Contrast 2's null is explicitly classified INCONCLUSIVE (underpowered), not a real negative. | |

KV per token (32B: 64 layers × 8 KV heads × 128 × K,V × bf16) = 256 KiB. **Offload buffer: 256 GiB pinned host memory (≈1.05M tokens, ≈16.6× the 62,944-token GPU KV).** SERVER (both configs): RH4's image server (`--limit-mm-per-prompt {"image": 640, "video": 0}`, RH3 `--mm-processor-kwargs`, max_model_len 32768, gmu 0.90, FA3, bf16 KV) + `--enable-prefix-caching --enable-prompt-tokens-details`; GPU+host adds the two offload flags. Server /metrics scraped every 1 s (all prefix-cache, external-prefix-cache, kv_offload, preemption, KV-usage, queue counters); host RSS of the server process tree and `free` recorded per cell. **PART A** (isolated, one request at a time; D2 format; greedy, escalation max_tokens 4): sizes ~11k tokens (history to t = 240 s, 4 sessions with duration ≥ 240 s) and ~28k (t = 618 s; **only 2 of 234 sessions are that long**, so 2 sessions — a deviation from "4 sessions", stated), 3 repetitions each (fresh header tag per repetition). Per repetition on the GPU+host server: append (history to t, max_tokens 1) → escalation = **GPU-WARM**; then filler histories from other sessions totalling ≥ 1.5× GPU KV → escalation = **HOST-WARM**. On the GPU-only server: append → escalation (GPU-WARM reference) → same fillers → escalation = **COLD**. **Verification per request from /metrics deltas (single request in flight):** GPU-WARM: local prefix hits ≥ 0.9× history tokens, external hits ≈ 0. HOST-WARM: local prefix hits ≤ 64 tokens (GPU copy gone), external (host) hits ≥ 0.9× history tokens, kv_offload_load_bytes > 0. COLD: cached_tokens ≤ 64. **STOP if any HOST-WARM request fails its verification.** Reported: TTFT median / min / max per state and size; reload bandwidth = load_bytes ÷ load_time (metric) and load_bytes ÷ (TTFT_host − TTFT_GPU-warm). **PART B** (fleet; RH2's harness, settings, plans and seeds: `study_rh2_knee_surface.plan/device`, 1× real-time, append every 10 s, 15-min window, warm-up upload, escalation rate 1.0, max_tokens 4; history = D2 format with RH2's per-cell/device/session header wording adapted to the 2-s stride): P-WARM, D ∈ {4, 8, 16, 24} × {GPU-only, GPU+host}, one repetition. Plans are RH5's construction dealt over D: no device is without sessions at any D, but at D = 64 / 96, 10 / 68 devices run out of sessions before the window ends (2–3 sessions each at D = 96) — kept in D, reported. **D\*** = smallest Part A D with warm escalation TTFT median > 1.5 s or p99 > 10 s; if none, D\* = 96 (stated). **PART B (levers at D\*):** B1 append every 30 s (D2); B2 format D1 (RH4: stride 2 s, 288×224, 65 tok/img; same header wording), append every 10 s; B3 lazy: no appends and no warm-up upload, each escalation sends the full D2 history from session start (prefix caching + offload on, so a device's own previous escalation in the same session can be reused) — B3 escalations are cold by design and are reported as escalation TTFT against the D\* baseline. **PART C:** D\*, D2, 10 s, `--kv-offloading-size 64`. **METRICS per cell:** warm escalation TTFT median/p90/p99/max; append TTFT median/p99; token hit; host share of hits (external ÷ (external + local) prefix-hit tokens); GPU KV usage median/max; PCIe load/store GB and average GB/s (kv_offload_load/store_bytes deltas over window + drain), plus the 1-s peak load rate; computed prompt tok/s **ACHIEVED** = Σ(prompt − cached) of requests completed between window start and drain end ÷ that elapsed time, and **DEMAND** = Σ(prompt − cached) of requests submitted in the window ÷ 900 s, separately; API-server CPU cores (median, max; busiest thread), engine tree cores, harness cores and submit lag p99; waiting max, preemptions, in flight at window end, drain time; errors with over-context HTTP 400s separate. **Host-tier miss evidence:** vLLM 0.31 exposes no host-eviction counter (source: `v1/kv_offload/cpu/manager.py`; `kv_offload_cpu_cache_usage_perc` is the fraction of host chunks pinned by in-flight transfers, not occupancy — this explains RH5's ≤ 0.026 reading); host capacity is evidenced by **warm misses** (an append/escalation whose cached tokens < 0.5× the prompt of the same device-session's previous completed request) and `kv_offload_allocation_failure`; analytic live-set peak per cell (Σ current history of devices in a session, 5-s grid) vs GPU+host capacity (1,111,520 tokens at 256 GiB; 325,088 at 64 GiB). **BINDING RESOURCE per cell (pre-registered evidence thresholds):** GPU running-set KV = KV usage max ≥ 0.95 AND (waiting max > 0 or preemptions > 0); GPU compute = achieved computed tok/s ≥ 4,000 (RH5's 4.5k / 3.8k COLD prefill and ~5k collapse rates), NVML SM util reported alongside; PCIe = average load ≥ 35 GB/s (70% of RH5's 48–53 GB/s reload rate) or 1-s peak ≥ 35 GB/s sustained ≥ 30 s; host capacity = warm misses > 5% of warm requests or allocation failures > 0; API-server CPU = API process median ≥ 0.9 core (or busiest thread ≥ 0.9). Harness validity: if harness submit lag p99 > 1 s the cell's load was not delivered as scheduled — reported, cell flagged. If no threshold is met but the cell breaches, "cannot attribute" is reported. **OFFLOAD VERIFICATION (STOP gate, every cell, RH5's counters):** whole-cell deltas: kv_offload_load_bytes ÷ external_prefix_cache_hits = 262,144 B/token ± 5% AND external hits > 0 (B3: ratio checked if host hits > 0, else "not exercised", not a failure). **DECISION RULES:** **KNEE:** report D\* and its binding resource; **NO KNEE IN RANGE** if no Part A cell breaches. **LEVERS MOVE THE KNEE:** at D\*, a lever is effective if warm escalation median ≤ 1.5 s AND p99 ≤ 10 s, or p99 cut ≥ 50% vs the D\* baseline (Part A cell at D\*); B1, B2, B3 reported separately; B3 additionally states its (cold) escalation TTFT against the D\* baseline. If the binding resource at D\* is one no lever affects, say so (narrows the controller claim). Part C: same metrics; reported as host-capacity sensitivity (no separate rule). **ESTIMATE** (`--stage estimate`; fluid model with RH5-measured rates: offload service time 0.275 s/request (M/M/1 fit to RH5 D24's 0.81-s median at 2.37 req/s → capacity 3.64 req/s), recompute at RH5's achieved ~5k tok/s when the live set exceeds GPU+host capacity, warm-up at 4.5k tok/s): Part A request rates 3.46 / 5.18 / 6.68 / 7.97 req/s, live-set peak 0.34 / 0.50 / 0.62 / 0.88× of 256-GiB capacity; Part C live set 1.15 / 1.72 / 2.13 / 3.01× of 64-GiB capacity at D\* = 32 / 48 / 64 / 96 (host thrash → long drains). Total **4.39 h for the model-predicted D\* = 32**; 5.66 / 6.38 / 7.27 / 6.97 h if D\* = 48 / 64 / 96 / none. All < 8 h → no drops. STOP if the estimate > 8 h (drop D = 96 first, then Part C) or if host-hit verification fails in any cell. Safety (not a result rule): chain halts if host MemAvailable < 150 GiB. **Mechanism verification (Step 1, pre-registered):** with offload, a warm append costs (i) API-server receipt and decode of the whole history's images (the protocol re-sends the full history), (ii) host→GPU reload of the prefix not resident on GPU (history × 256 KiB over PCIe), (iii) GPU compute for the new frames, and (iv) running-set GPU KV for the full history while the request runs; per-device load ∝ (1 / cadence) × history size, and the knee sits where one of (i)–(iv) or host capacity saturates (FORMULATION.md: physical inertia grows with context; warm-state capacity). B1 cuts the request rate 3× (all of (i), (ii), (iv) per unit time) at equal new-token compute; B2 cuts bytes per token by ~29% ((ii), (iv), host capacity) and image bytes, not the request count or image count; B3 removes appends and converts warm state into cold escalations (compute per escalation). Step 4 control: RH5's GPU-only cells (offload disabled; committed) and the RH5 D ≤ 24 cells below the knee. PASS iff (a) prompt token ids identical from the first image onward and prompt_tokens equal for FULL/FULLU/DELTA at every step, and DELTA cached_tokens == FULLU cached_tokens at every step; (b) every DELTA append body <= 1.2 x (new-frame fragments + text + JSON envelope) and <= 10 % of the FULL body; (c) mean API-server CPU-seconds per append DELTA <= 0.5 x FULL; negative control: a uuid never sent -> HTTP 400 cache miss. TARGETS: (a) device incorrect; (b) escalation gain g = cloud_correct - device_correct in {-1, 0, +1}, ranked by predicted gain; (c) S-EMBER only, evidence outside the recent window (R1 K3 `evidence_covered` == false). Baselines: random (mean of 200 draws, seed 0); query-only router; final-answer confidence (ERQA option-letter probabilities from r4; S-EMBER has none and is reported as not available); full-trace learned router; perfect oracle. Paired CIs for every router MINUS the query-only router. Decision latency at k = the Orin's median time to the k-th reasoning token (TTFT + the k-th token timestamp from `tokens_4bt_f16_F16.jsonl.gz`); query-only = 0 s. Main figure: accuracy at e = 0.2 against decision latency in seconds, one point per (router, k), with always-local, always-cloud and random reference lines; short axis labels, no long caption. **PART 3 — rescue matrix (S-EMBER):** per question, history-rescuable (device: C1 wrong and C2 right; cloud: K3 wrong and K1 right), capability-rescuable (C1 wrong and K3 right), both-needed (C1, C2 and K3 wrong and K1 right), none. Counts and pairwise overlaps, and each Part 1 signal's AUROC for history-rescuable versus capability-rescuable AMONG the questions the device fails. **GATE (pre-registered, identical for R8a and R8a-v2):** PARTIAL REASONING ADDS VALUE iff, held out, the best query+prefix router at some k <= 100 reasoning tokens beats the query-only router at escalation budget e = 0.2 by >= 2 pp with a 95% CI excluding 0, on S-EMBER or ERQA (both reported, each with its own CI; the gate fires if either does). Otherwise NOT SHOWN, and the report must state which leg failed: detection (held-out AUROC of the prefix router for device-incorrect not above the query-only router's), utility (accuracy at e = 0.2 not above by 2 pp), or both. **Mechanism verification (Step 1, written before any scoring).** The claim is that the reasoning prefix carries decision-relevant information not present in the query. Chain: (i) the device's 4B-Thinking trace contains surface markers of the failure modes that escalation can fix (hesitation, repetition, answer churn, explicit statements that evidence is missing from the window) - this is what `reasoning_signals` already measures and what FORMULATION.md's semantic-inertia component predicts, since a pruned recent window removes evidence the model then reports as absent; (ii) those markers are measurable from the FIRST k tokens, before the answer exists, so a router can act on them at a decision latency below the full trace; (iii) ranking by them separates questions where the cloud recovers the answer (g = +1) from those where it does not; (iv) escalating the top-e by that ranking therefore beats escalating the top-e by a query-only ranking at the same budget. Step 2 instantiates each link with the named quantity and must show it is non-zero and varies: if any prefix feature is constant across questions at a given k (for example every trace closed before k = 25, so `trace_closed_by_k` is constant), that k is marked CONSTANT for that feature and reported, not silently averaged. Step 3 traces one question at each k end to end (prefix text, every feature value, predicted gain, fold, rank, whether it was escalated at e = 0.2, device and cloud outcome). Step 4 negative control: shuffle the prefix-feature matrix across questions WITHIN each training fold (destroying the question-to-prefix link while keeping the marginal distributions) and confirm the prefix router collapses to the query-only router; and a second control fitting on prefixes of length 0, which must equal the query-only router by construction. Step 5 alternative explanation: a prefix router could win merely because longer traces correlate with harder questions, which the query-only router cannot see but a trace-length feature can - distinguished by also fitting a LENGTH-ONLY router (number of reasoning tokens produced by k, plus `trace_closed_by_k`) and reporting it beside the full prefix router; if the full router does not beat the length-only router, the finding is reported as trace length, not trace content. **R8a-v2:** when R8b and R8c finish, R8a is re-run unchanged with two added feature groups - per-step entropy and chosen-token logprob statistics over the prefix (mean, max, last, count above the training-fold median) from R8b/R8c - and with repeated-sample labels (per question, the number of correct samples across the original cell and the two new seeds) used as a SECONDARY, better-powered target for (a); the primary target stays the single recorded outcome.
