Controls behaved: the k=0 prefix reproduces query-only exactly (delta 0.00, CI [0.00, 0.00], AUROC delta 0.0000) and the fold-shuffled prefix collapses to -0.24 pp [-1.31, +0.61], AUROC -0.0264 [-0.0383, -0.0136]. The frozen MiniLM embedding router is WORSE than query-only on S-EMBER (-0.1044 [-0.1543, -0.0544]) and is reported, not dropped. Five pre-registered missing-evidence phrases never fire at any k on either workload and are reported as written. By Rule 2 this is a **real negative** for utility at k<=100 (no ABSENT link; controls moved the result) and a real positive for S-EMBER detection. Report: reports/study_r8a_prefix_signals.md. **v2 PENDING** StudyR8b and StudyR8c. **v2 RESULT (2026-10-24), after R8b/R8c supplied per-token uncertainty:** device arm necessarily changed to R8b/R8c seed 1 (uncertainty belongs to one generation; seed-1 logprobs cannot be attached to a seed-0 trace), so v2 was run on BOTH seeds. **The gate FIRES on each v2 seed by the letter of the pre-registered rule and does NOT replicate:** seed 1 `query+prefix_hand_k50` **+2.012 pp** [0.369, 3.114]; seed 2 `query+prefix_hand_k100` **+2.130 pp** [0.228, 3.345] - a DIFFERENT cell, and each winner is null in the other two samples (hand_k50: -0.24 / +2.01 / +0.71; hand_k100: +0.95 / +0.83 / +2.13 across v1 / s1 / s2). Resampling the device alone moves the statistic by more than the 2 pp the gate asks for, so the threshold sits inside its own noise floor; the winning router moreover contains NO uncertainty features, and the gate's search family grew from 24 to 40 candidate cells per workload with no multiplicity or replication requirement. The better-powered expected-device-accuracy metric shows the same instability and never reaches 2 pp. **The gate is reported as written, NOT redefined post hoc; on the evidence partial reasoning should not be treated as shown, and whether to amend the registry with a replication requirement is the user's call.** **The uncertainty signal adds nothing - a REAL NEGATIVE** (mechanism present: features vary, and R8c's Step-4 control collapses entropy 22x): `prefix_unc` alone is WORSE than query-only on both workloads (S-EMBER s1 -0.0507 [-0.1002, -0.0023]; ERQA s2 -0.0796 [-0.1537, -0.0058]) and the only uncertainty results significant in both seeds are negative. **What DOES replicate:** detecting evidence-outside-window from the prefix alone at **k=50** - AUROC delta +0.1418 [0.066, 0.217] (v1), +0.1290 [0.061, 0.204] (s1), +0.0670 [-0.007, 0.145] (s2) against a query-only router at chance (0.474); v1's headline k=100 figure does NOT survive (+0.1506 -> +0.0705 -> -0.0348), so k=50 is the honest anchor. Signal is in the hand-crafted text features, not uncertainty. k=0 control exact (delta 0.000, CI [0,0]); shuffled-prefix control collapses on the evidence target in all 3 samples. A full v1 re-run after the v2 code changes reproduced the canonical v1 JSON exactly (one added key, all numbers within 1e-9). **CORRECTION (audit 2026-11-03) — TARGET MISMATCH.** The `evidence_outside_window` figures above are target-mismatched: `study_r8a_prefix_signals.py:587` sets `sc = r["fail"]` for every target, so every evidence-window AUROC is a DEVICE-FAILURE model scored on the evidence label; no router was ever fitted on that label. SCOPE: PREP + 50-item pilot per dataset only; NO main sweep. ARMS (same data window, same question text except the clause naming the input form): TEXT = numeric series inline with stated precision and channel names; PLOT = fixed-format line plot PNG (identical axes style for every item). DATASETS/LICENCES (all verified open, no account): STARK `prquan/STARK_10k` + `prquan/STARK_1k` (BSD-3-Clause, code `github.com/nesl/STARK_Benchmark`, 14,552 items over 26 task families, `query`/`answer` columns used VERBATIM - no new questions); WISDM UCI id 507 (CC BY 4.0, DOI 10.24432/C5HK59, 51 subjects, 18 activities, phone accel 20 Hz); MIT-BIH `physionet.org/content/mitdb/1.0.0` (Open Data Commons Attribution v1.0, DOI 10.13026/C2F305, 48 records, 360 Hz, MLII+V5). OUR OWN FORMULATIONS (marked as such in the report; STARK's are the benchmark's own): WISDM = 18-way activity classification over one 10 s / 200-sample phone-accel window per item, 3 channels (x,y,z m/s^2, 2 dp), chance 5.56 %, subject-disjoint reporting; MIT-BIH = 5-way AAMI EC57 beat classification (N/S/V/F/Q) over one 1.0 s / 360-sample MLII window centred on the annotated R peak (mV, 3 dp), chance 20 %. PILOT: seed 0, 50 items per dataset stratified by class/tier, Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct (snap `ebb281ec70b05090aa6165b016eac8ec08e71b17`) on A6000 and, if free, Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct (snap `0c351dd01ed87e9c1b53cbc748cba10e6187ff3b`) on RTX 3090 Ti; greedy, max_new_tokens 512, both arms. STACK DEVIATION FLAGGED PRE-RUN: flash's only vLLM is 0.8.5, whose registry has NO `Qwen3VLForConditionalGeneration` (max is `Qwen2_5_VLForConditionalGeneration`), so vLLM CANNOT serve Qwen3-VL on this host; the pilot would use HF transformers 5.12.1 / torch 2.4.1+cu118 (the StudyR8c stack) and the user was asked before running. METRICS: accuracy + Wilson 95 %, always-majority baseline, chance, prompt-token median/max, failure and empty-answer rates, per dataset x arm; indicative only at n=50. STARK METRIC CAVEAT: only its categorical families (`landmark_questions` binary, `direction_questions` 8-way, topological-relation families 0/1) support accuracy; the continuous families (`loc_*`, `*_impute`, `spatiotemporal_forecast`, `loc_bearing`) are scored RMSE/RMSPE by the paper and have no accuracy, majority or chance baseline. STOP CONDITIONS: prompt over model length (no truncation or downsampling without asking), no usable labels, or any task needing a definition change. | `experiments/ts/study_d12_prep.py` (prep + pilot; written before any run) | STARK HF `prquan/STARK_10k`; WISDM UCI 507; MIT-BIH mitdb 1.0.0. Answer only Yes or No."; no times, names or elapsed time in the prompt (verified on one traced prompt). **Models:** Qwen3-VL-4B-I @ebb281ec, 32B-I @0cfaf481, D-series vLLM stack, greedy, max_tokens 8, logprobs; prefix + processor cache off; batched (accuracy only, no latency reported). **Score:** P(Yes) normalised over Yes/No token variants at the first generated token; rate of neither reported (STOP if > 1 %). **Ground truth:** positive = positive clip with prefix end ≥ time_of_alert; negative = negative clip, any offset; positive clips with prefix end < time_of_alert UNSCORED (early-response Yes rate reported). **Controls:** uniform-grey frames (100 requests per model per resolution; STOP if Yes rate ≈ 1 or ≈ real-frame rate); causality assert per request; identical-input check across models (frame hashes, prompt tokens). **Baselines:** always-Yes; scene/weather/light logistic regression and optical-flow-feature logistic regression (CPU, same 9 SMALL frames), both out-of-fold by clip, AUROC; reference-time distribution check within duration bins. **Analysis:** per model × resolution × offset AUROC and accuracy at 0.5 (cluster bootstrap by clip, 2,000, seed 0), also within Urban and Highway; Yes rates, FPR, 4B-vs-32B disagreement, tokens; 32B − 4B AUROC per offset (indicative); 5 listed examples per agreement cell (file + offset only). SUPPORTED iff 95 % CI excludes 0 and estimate > 0; otherwise NOT SUPPORTED, null classified under CLAUDE.md Rule 2. **Sanity gate (passed before registration of the run):** this code on D2-pilot SMALL reproduces slow +0.0061, sudden +0.1066, difference +0.1004 on 36 / 39 clips. **Clips:** every DEV clip not in the D2 pilot (~601); pilot clips never in a confirmatory result; no val / test frame read. | StudyE6-intervention | orchestration | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-11-10, before any computation (user-authorized row). OFFLINE ANALYSIS OF EXISTING RESULTS; CPU only; no new inference; no model server; Instruct only, greedy; no INVALID result used.** Question: using only information available BEFORE each question, can a policy predict which intervention improves the answer (larger model / richer evidence / both / neither), and does a combined policy beat a strong independent evidence selector plus model router under the same resources? Cells: H1 (4B RECENT), H2 (4B RICH), K3 (32B RECENT), K1 (32B RICH) on S-EMBER 845, H100 vLLM 0.31.0; secondary D11 arm U, RH4 D2. PART 0 (pre-specified): E4's per-question upload rule for cloud-held history arms (RICH/U/D2) is changed to the session rule (continuous upload, only the newest frame pending at question time); RECENT keeps full per-question upload. E4 tables 4.1 and 5 recomputed under this rule and reported BESIDE the originals. ASSUMPTION stated: RICH re-samples frames at each question (StudyRH3), so a cloud holding all frames at 1 fps can build it. TARGETS per question from the 1/0 correctness of the four cells: capability@recent = K3-H1; evidence@4B = H2-H1; both = K1-H1; capability@rich = K1-H2; evidence@32B = K1-K3; joint distribution reported. NOISE FLOOR: D11 arm F vs K3 and F vs H1 (E4 s6). FEATURES, available before the question only, no answers/correctness/model outputs as features: F0 none; F1 category + question time + qt bin; F2 F1 + question text TF-IDF (vocabulary fitted in training folds only); F3 F2 + session history (count, categories, times of earlier questions in the same video). Optional F4 adds StudyD13 4B-RECENT answer-letter logprobs, with the 4B run charged. POLICIES, one of the four cells per question: oracle; best static; per-type (category x qt bin, as E4); INDEPENDENT BASELINE = R8a-style coverage predictor on F2 (threshold fitted in training folds) choosing RECENT/RICH, plus a model router choosing 4B/32B by (a) always 32B, (b) 4B confidence threshold fitted in training folds, (c) F2 classifier for K3>H1 - all three reported, strongest is the baseline; capability-only; evidence-only; COMBINED (predicts correctness of all four cells, picks best under constraints). Logistic regression AND gradient-boosted trees both reported. 5-fold cross-fitting by video, seed 0, nothing tuned on test folds. SETTINGS: (a) accuracy under budgets, fraction to 32B in {0.25,0.5,1.0} x fraction using RICH in {0.25,0.5,1.0}; (b) timely utility with Part 0 costs at 88 Mbit/s PNG 5 s and 10 s and 13.6 Mbit/s PNG 10 s. PRE-SPECIFIED INTERPRETATION RULES (fixed before computing, no rules added after): combined beats independent ONLY IF the paired CI excludes 0 in at least one pre-specified setting AND is not below 0 in the others; if F2/F3 add nothing over F1 the decision reduces to a per-type lookup rule; capability prediction counts as useful ONLY IF capability-only beats always-32B and always-4B within its budget. | StudyE6b-mobility | orchestration | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-11-11, before any computation (user-authorized row). STAGE A ONLY. OFFLINE REPLAY OF COMMITTED RESULTS; CPU only on the flash host; no GPU, no model server, no inference; Instruct only, greedy; no INVALID result used (E24/E24b/E24c, E31 Parts C/D, I2/I3 TEMPORAL/SPATIAL, D/D2 Thinking, K/J Thinking T0.6 all excluded).** QUESTION: when questions arrive over time and the network drops out, does deciding WHEN to keep which tier warm (device history, cloud history) improve timely correct answers over (i) the best static configuration and (ii) a decomposed policy that chooses model and evidence as in StudyE6 setting (a) and placement only at question time, and at what keep-warm cost? COST CORRECTIONS (pre-specified, fixing three errors in StudyE4 and StudyE6 setting (b), all of which favoured 32B-RICH): (1) RECENT was charged a full on-demand upload per question while cloud-held history arms were charged as streamed - one deployment mode is now applied to ALL cloud arms and never mixed; (2) the 4B cold latency curve ended at 12,501 tokens (image-format runs only) so 4B-RICH counted unavailable on 471 of 845 questions - replaced by StudyF3's per-format 4B curves (video COLD 1,810-22,387 tokens); (3) the 32B cold curve pooled image and video runs in 100-token bins so K3 got image pricing and K1 video pricing - replaced by StudyF3's separate per-format, per-cache-state curves. DEPLOYMENT MODES, one per run, never mixed: STREAM (device uploads each arm's frames continuously at that arm's frame rate while connected; at question time only not-yet-uploaded frames are pending; during an outage frames queue and the backlog uploads after reconnection before any later question can be served; per-hour uplink MB charged even when no question arrives) and ON-DEMAND (the full input uploads at question time). CONFIGURATIONS, accuracy per question from committed results only: cloud 4B and 32B x RECENT (H1/K3), RICH (H2/K1), U (StudyD11), D2 (StudyRH4 32B cold and warm); device (Jetson Orin 4B) short window N=1/3/9 (StudyF4 letters and StudyF4 Part B latency), RECENT 120 s cold (StudyF1/StudyClean H1 letters as proxy, FLAGGED; StudyD4-orin/DevFast cold curve). PENDING, listed and never estimated until StudyE7 and StudyE8 are committed: cloud 4B-D2, device D2 warm and cold, device keep-warm energy. A prompt outside a measured token range is UNKNOWN: counts reported per configuration, primary analysis treats UNKNOWN as unavailable, with a labelled linear-extrapolation sensitivity. NETWORK: outages are Irish 5G cell_id = -1 seconds (E31b's valid signal, never State=D/I); regimes (1) outdoor-driving Irish sessions, (2) indoor herolab RSSI below -75 dBm from the per-sample datalog time series, (3) synthetic two-state sensitivity using Irish's empirical gap-duration distribution at outage time fractions 0, 2, 5, 10, 20 and 30 percent, labelled synthetic. Connected uplink fixed at 5, 13.6 and 88 Mbit/s with RTT 60, 44 and 22 ms; uplink variation is NOT modelled and the Irish traces are download. ALIGNMENT: each S-EMBER video is mapped to a random window of a trace session, 20 offsets per video (seeds 0-19), questions replayed in time order within each video. DEADLINES 1, 2, 5 and 10 s; utility = correct AND delivered within the deadline; an unreachable cloud at question time means only device configurations can answer. POLICIES, STAGE A: oracle (per-question best configuration, upper bound); best static configuration per regime x uplink x deadline, chosen on training offsets; P1 decomposed / no pre-warm (model and evidence from StudyE6 setting (a)'s best rule, cloud STREAM when connected, and when the cloud is unreachable the best device configuration available without pre-warming). STAGE B, only when the user says StudyE7 and StudyE8 are committed: P2 always-warm device, P3 connectivity-aware keep-warm (device warm only while predicted outage risk over the next H seconds exceeds theta, predictor on past trace seconds only, theta and H fitted on training trace sessions, cross-fit by trace session 5 folds seed 0), P3-noncausal sanity, oracle keep-warm, both negative controls and the decision rule. NEGATIVE CONTROLS: (a) outage fraction 0 - P1 must never fall back to the device and must equal the always-cloud decomposed policy (STAGE A); (b) device-warm accuracy set to device short-window accuracy - P2/P3 gains must collapse (STAGE B). PRE-SPECIFIED DECISION RULE, fixed before computing and not added to afterwards (STAGE B): report P3 - P1 and P2 - P1 utility (paired, video-and-offset bootstrap, 2,000 resamples, seed 0) and P3 vs P2 device energy per hour; the keep-warm decision is NON-TRIVIAL only if P3 matches P2's utility (difference CI within plus/minus 1 pp) at significantly lower device energy, or beats P1 with a CI excluding 0, in at least one pre-specified cell (outdoor-driving or synthetic at or above 5 percent, uplink 13.6 Mbit/s, deadline 2 or 5 s); if P2 equals P1 everywhere, device-warm has no value on this workload; if P3 equals P2 at equal energy, the decision is a static rule. The fraction of utility differences arising from questions during outages vs while connected is reported. Every comparison states whether input, stack and decoding match. Observations are separated from interpretation. P3-noncausal, given the true future, chooses warm fraction 1.000 and reproduces P2 (0.3840 vs 0.3842) rather than improving on it, so this is NOT a prediction failure: Irish outages are p50 1 s / max 3 s while a mid-session warm-up is a full cold prefill (20.6 s at 10k tokens), so no schedule can warm up inside a gap. | StudyE9-episodes | orchestration | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-11-11, before any computation (user-authorized row). OFFLINE ANALYSIS OF COMMITTED RESULTS; CPU only on the flash host; no GPU, no model server, no inference. No INVALID result used.** QUESTION: in a device-triggered cloud escalation design (device 4B scores every second; when its score crosses a trigger threshold the cloud 32B scores each following second until it alarms or dismisses), does the cloud's greater capability shorten escalation episodes and reduce total cloud work - including re-escalations after wrong dismissals - compared with resolving the same episodes with 4B, at matched false-alarm rates? And how much of always-cloud's predictive quality does escalation keep per unit of cloud work? METRIC RULE (fixed, not to be changed): predictive quality is the Nexar metric - AP at fixed horizons h in {0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0} s before the event, computed on CONTINUOUS scores (log P(Yes)) exactly as StudyE5 Part G (positives = each positive clip's score at the whole second nearest event - h, clamped to its grid; negatives = all 5,196 negative-clip decision seconds; base rate 0.028). Literal Yes/No answers are NEVER used anywhere; every application action (alarm, dismiss, trigger) is a threshold on the continuous score. DATA: `results/dashcam/study_d14_trace/d14_trace_h100nvl.jsonl.gz`, 300 Nexar VALIDATION clips (150 pos / 150 neg), one score per arm x clip x second (t = 3..floor(duration)), arms 4BI N1/N3 and 32BI N1/N3/N9. Device arm = 4BI N1 (the Orin sustains N=1 at 1 Hz, StudyD8 arm A); 4BI N3 only as a LABELLED SECONDARY because the Orin cannot sustain it at 1 Hz. EPISODE MODEL: a trigger (device score >= tau_trig) opens an episode at second t0 if none is open; the resolver (32B at N in {1,3,9}; counterfactual resolver 4B N1, the same model as the device) scores every second from t0; ALARM when a resolver score >= tau_alarm(model, N); DISMISS when resolver scores are < tau_dismiss(model, N) for k consecutive seconds, k in {1,2,3}; CAP L_max = 10 s (dismiss at cap; sensitivity 5 and 20 s); clip end closes the episode. After a dismissal the device keeps monitoring and a later trigger opens a NEW episode (re-escalation), with cooldown c in {0, 2} s. THRESHOLDS per (model, N), fitted by 2-fold cross-fitting by clip stratified by label, seed 0, evaluated on the held-out fold and pooled: tau_alarm at per-second false-alarm rates on training-fold negative seconds of {1%, 5%, 10%}; tau_dismiss at the training-fold negative score quantile of {50%, 75%}; tau_trig at device trigger rates on training-fold negative seconds of {5%, 10%, 20%, 40%} (the load knob). TIMING: a resolver score for second t is available at t + latency; the integer rule is d = ceil(latency) seconds, so the score the application sees at wall second t is the one computed from second t - d, and seconds with t - d < 3 are marked unavailable and ranked below every observed score (counts reported). TWO LATENCY SETTINGS, labelled everywhere: (i) ZERO (pure capability); (ii) MEASURED - device = StudyD8 arm A sustained 1 Hz N=1 total latency p50 0.567 s (proxy flag: DEV-RECENT payload, not Nexar SMALL); cloud = 0.044 s RTT + upload of N PNG frames at 13.6 Mbit/s using StudyD4 frame bytes (N=1 354,902 B; N=3 1,057,532 B; N=9 3,188,243 B) + 32B open-loop TTFT p50 from StudyD7b (N=1) and StudyD7 (N=3, N=9) at 25% and 75% of peak load, both reported. POLICIES on the same clips with the same threshold procedure: device-only (4B N1 every second, its own tau_alarm, zero cloud work); always-cloud (32B at N every second, no device trigger); escalation-32B (device trigger, 32B resolver); escalation-4B counterfactual (device trigger, 4B N1 resolver with its own thresholds), which isolates capability at matched trigger and matched alarm false-alarm rate. OUTPUTS per policy x setting: (1) AP at each horizon on the score stream the application actually sees (resolver score inside episodes, device score outside), clip-bootstrap CIs 2,000 seed 0 stratified by label and shared across cells, plus device-only and always-cloud AP, plus the AP-CURVE HORIZON SHIFT between 32B and 4B arms (interpolated, bootstrap CI) which is to be reported explicitly as a CURVE SHIFT, NOT a per-event time; (2) alarm timing - per positive clip the alarm time relative to the event, fraction alarmed at or before event - h at each horizon, and false alarms per negative clip-hour; (3) cloud work - resolver invocations and GPU-seconds per episode (requests x prompt tokens / measured saturated prefill rate: 32B ~5.6k tok/s from StudyD7, 4B ~29.5k tok/s from StudyD7b), reported separately for positive and negative clips, and per clip-hour including re-escalations, and the same for always-cloud; (4) episode outcomes - alarm / dismiss / cap / clip-end counts, false dismissals (dismissed on a positive clip before the event and the event then occurs), re-escalations per clip, and time from first trigger to final resolution. PRIMARY CONFIGURATION for the AP bootstrap grid, fixed now: k = 2, tau_dismiss quantile 75%, L_max = 10 s, cooldown c = 0; k, dismiss quantile, L_max and cooldown are swept for all episode and cloud-work outputs and reported as sensitivities. PRE-SPECIFIED DECISION RULES, fixed before computing and not added to afterwards: CAPABILITY SHORTENS EPISODES if escalation-32B has lower total cloud invocations per clip-hour than escalation-4B at matched tau_trig and matched alarm FPR, with a paired clip-bootstrap CI excluding 0, in at least 2 of the 3 alarm FPR levels under latency setting (ii) - positives and negatives reported separately with a statement of which dominates load. ESCALATION IS EFFICIENT if it keeps AP within 0.01 of always-cloud at h <= 1 s with overlapping CIs while using <= 50% of always-cloud's cloud work, at some tau_trig; the AP-vs-cloud-work frontier over tau_trig is reported. If neither holds, that is stated plainly and no rule is added. NEGATIVE CONTROLS: (a) replacing the 32B resolver scores with 4B N1 scores must make escalation-32B equal escalation-4B exactly; (b) shuffling resolver scores across seconds within each clip must destroy alarm timing relative to the event, so AP at h <= 1 s falls toward the base rate. Mechanism verification Steps 1-5 recorded BEFORE the sweep. One instrumented episode trace (a positive clip with a false dismissal and re-escalation, if any). Observations separated from interpretation. Anything beyond this row is exploratory. **AMENDMENT 2026-11-11 (user-authorized): (1) report correction — the earliness wording in report s3.1 and s15 cited StudyE5's within-clip rise analysis as evidence about earliness; under the project's decided metric rule earliness on Nexar is read only from the Nexar metric (AP-curve horizon shift on continuous scores), so both sentences were replaced by that statement and no Yes/No-commitment or within-clip-rise analysis is cited anywhere in the report. (2) LATENCY SETTING (iii) JPEG UPLOAD, SENSITIVITY ONLY, added as report s16: setting (ii) recomputed with StudyD4's JPEG q90 request bytes (59,601/177,773/529,361 B at N=1/3/9), same RTT, same 13.6 Mbit/s uplink, same server TTFT and device latency, same d=ceil(latency); MODEL SCORES ARE THE PNG SCORES and JPEG accuracy parity is PENDING StudyE10a, so (iii) is unvalidated and no conclusion rests on it. d_cloud changes in 3 of 8 arm x load cells (32B N=3 @75% 2s->1s, 32B N=9 @25% 3s->1s, 32B N=9 @75% 4s->3s); rule 1 flips to MET in 3 of 24 cells (25% N=9 trigger 5% and 20%, 75% N=3 trigger 20%) and rule 2 is met in 27 of 72 cells against 18 of the same 72 under (ii). (i) and (ii) numbers are unchanged byte-for-byte: (iii) is written to `results/dashcam/study_e9/e9_results_jpeg.json` and `e9_results.json` was not rewritten. New-number consistency check PASS 6/6 in report s16.5, with check 1 reproducing every unchanged-d_cloud cell of (ii) exactly. (3) Added a limitation note to report s13: d=ceil(latency) on a 1-s grid sets cloud and device delay equal at N=1 (0.395 s and 0.567 s both become 1 s), removing the cloud's latency advantage there; a sub-second trace would be needed to measure it. A DESIGN PROPERTY THAT BOUNDS THE EFFECT WAS RECORDED BEFORE THE SWEEP: because tau_alarm and tau_dismiss are quantiles of EACH MODEL'S OWN negative-second distribution, both resolvers alarm and fall below dismiss on the same fraction of negative seconds by construction, so any capability effect must come from within-clip ordering and from positive clips - which is why positives and negatives are reported separately. Step 4: control (a) PASSES EXACTLY in 36/36 cells; control (b) PASSES under latency setting (i) (AP at h=0 falls 0.133/0.161/0.136 to 0.053/0.045/0.041 against a 0.0281 base rate) and is UNINFORMATIVE under setting (ii), where the latency shift had already removed the timing signal before the control was applied. Step 5 names five alternative explanations and the column that separates each. RULE 2: both decision rules returned positive findings, so no null classification is required; the one null-like component (control (b) under setting (ii)) is INCONCLUSIVE, not a real negative, and is labelled so. | — |
| StudyE12-admission | orchestration | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-11-11 (later), before any computation (user-authorized row). OFFLINE ANALYSIS OF COMMITTED RESULTS; CPU only on the flash host; no GPU, no model server, no inference. No INVALID result used.** QUESTION: when cloud capacity admits only a fraction of device-triggered escalations, can a predictor built from the device's own score trajectory choose which escalations to admit better than admitting by the device score alone? METRIC RULE (decided, not varied): Nexar AP at h = 0/0.5/1/1.5/2 s on CONTINUOUS scores (log P(Yes)), exactly as StudyE5 Part G and StudyE9; no literal Yes/No answer anywhere; alarm, dismiss and trigger are thresholds on continuous scores. SETUP: StudyD14 trace, 300 validation clips (150 pos / 150 neg); device 4BI N=1; resolver 32B at N=1 and N=3, both reported; StudyE9's primary episode configuration (k=2, dismiss quantile 75%, L_max 10 s, cooldown 0) and its threshold procedure (2-fold cross-fit by clip, stratified by label, seed 0); alarm FPR 5% primary with 1% and 10% secondary; trigger rate 20% primary with 10% secondary; latency setting (ii) at 25% load primary, (i) secondary. ADMISSION: each trigger is admitted (32B resolver runs as in E9) or kept local (the device's own 4B score continues and alarms on its own tau_alarm); budget B in {0.25, 0.5, 0.75} enforced per fold by a ranking-score cutoff fitted on TRAINING clips to admit B of training triggers, with the realized test fraction reported. TRAINING TARGETS (training clips only, used for ranking only, never for evaluation): per-episode utility U = 1[alarm on a positive clip at least h* = 0.5 s before the event] - lambda * (false alarms in the episode), lambda = 150/5196 = 0.0288684, sensitivity lambda x {0.5, 1, 2}; benefit = U(admit) - U(local); remaining work = resolver invocations until the episode closes including re-escalations within 10 s. FEATURES (strictly causal, trigger second only): device score at t; mean/max/least-squares slope/std over the last 3 and 5 s; number of earlier triggers in the clip and counts of earlier episode outcomes (alarm/dismiss/cap) so far; the current setting's d_cloud. EXCLUDED: elapsed time since clip start or since the standing query began (event timing is fixed relative to the Nexar clip, so it would leak the label); clip metadata excluded from the primary predictor, allowed only as a labelled secondary. POLICIES at equal budget and equal action space: P-random (mean over 20 seeds), P-score (device score at t, the baseline), P-trajectory (logistic regression on the features for benefit > 0), P-trajectory-work (predicted benefit / predicted work), P-oracle (true benefit, upper bound), references admit-all (= E9 escalation-32B) and admit-none (= E9 device-only). DECISION RULE (pre-registered): the trajectory signal is useful if P-trajectory or P-trajectory-work beats P-score on AP at h = 0.5 s with a paired clip-bootstrap CI excluding 0 at 2 of 3 budgets in the primary configuration at 32B N=1 or N=3, WITHOUT increasing cloud GPU-s by more than 10%; if not, the device score alone is the admission signal and the report says so plainly; if P-score does not beat P-random, admission ranking has no signal on this workload. NEGATIVE CONTROLS: (a) budget 1.0 - every policy must equal admit-all exactly; (b) features shuffled across triggers within a fold - P-trajectory must fall to P-random's level. Predictor AUROC and work R^2 are diagnostics only, never a conclusion. STOP AND ASK IF a feature would need information after the trigger second, the budget cannot be enforced as specified, or E9's numbers do not reproduce. | `experiments/dashcam/study_e12_admission.py`; `experiments/dashcam/study_e12_report.py` | `results/dashcam/study_d14_trace/d14_trace_h100nvl.jsonl.gz` (scores, labels, t, time_of_event, duration); `results/dashcam/study_e9/e9_results.json` (reference cells for check 1 and negative control (a)); StudyD4/D7/D7b/D8 latency constants via `experiments/dashcam/study_e9_episodes.py` | `results/dashcam/study_e12/`; `reports/study_e12_admission.md` | flash (CPU only; no GPU, no server, no inference) | 300 clips (150 pos / 150 neg), 10,660 clip-seconds per arm; 2 resolvers x 3 alarm FPR x 2 trigger rates x 2 latency settings x 4 budgets (incl. 1.0) x 7 policies; P-random 20 seeds; 2-fold threshold and predictor cross-fitting by clip seed 0; clip bootstrap 2,000 seed 0 stratified by label | **COMPLETE 2026-11-11 (later): THE DEVICE SCORE ALONE IS THE ADMISSION SIGNAL, AND ON AP NO ADMISSION RANKING SEPARATES FROM RANDOM.** The pre-registered decision rule is NOT MET: neither P-trajectory nor P-trajectory-work beats P-score on AP at h = 0.5 s with a paired clip-bootstrap CI excluding 0 at 2 of 3 budgets, for either resolver (best 0/3; the largest point gain is +0.0022 at B = 0.25, 32B N = 1, CI [-0.0021, +0.0099]). The STRONGER pre-registered null also fires: P-score beats P-random with a CI excluding 0 in only 1 of 6 resolver x budget cells (point estimates +0.0006 to +0.0060 at h = 0, all favouring P-score, none separating), so the pre-registered wording is adopted - ADMISSION RANKING HAS NO SIGNAL ON THIS WORKLOAD as measured by AP. **This is a REAL NEGATIVE, not a design failure (CLAUDE.md Rule 2):** the trajectory predictor was fitted and cross-fitted and DOES separate the training target out of sample (AUROC for benefit > 0 0.667-0.734; work model train R^2 0.157-0.198), negative control (b) removes that separation, and negative control (a) confirms the budget is the only thing the policies differ in. **Why a working predictor moves nothing, recorded in the mechanism verification BEFORE the sweep (Step 2):** at h = 0.5 s StudyE9's committed escalation-32B (0.0411) is already marginally BEHIND device-only (0.0416) at this cell, the two endpoints are only 0.0005 apart at the pre-registered horizon, so nothing is available by INTERPOLATING between them - but selection is not interpolation and P-oracle at a quarter budget reaches 0.0461, +0.0045 above admit-none and +0.0050 above admit-all on 31% of its GPU-seconds, so the headroom is real and of the same order as the effect the rule asked for; what fails is that no causal policy finds any of it. At h = 0 there IS headroom (+0.0126) and that column is reported, but it was not the pre-registered horizon and no rule is rewritten after the fact. **What admission does buy is cloud work and alarm behaviour, not AP:** at B = 0.50 with 32B N = 1, P-score reaches 0.0578 AP at h = 0 (39% of the way from admit-none to admit-all) for 43% of admit-all's GPU-seconds, and it raises 79.7 false alarms per clip-hour against P-random's 115.6 at the same budget while alarming on 0.467 of positive clips against 0.558 - a large, repeatable selection effect that AP, computed on the continuous score stream and not on the alarm decisions, does not see. A budget-respecting P-oracle scores 0.0477 at h = 0.5 s against P-score's 0.0420, so the headroom exists (+0.0057) and is not reachable from the device's own trajectory. ONE DEPARTURE FROM THE PRE-REGISTERED PROCEDURE, reported in the report body: the benefit label is discrete and mostly exactly 0, so a plain quantile cutoff admitted every trigger at or above 0 and the budget was NOT enforced for P-oracle (realized 0.917 at every budget); a deterministic per-(clip, second) tiebreak (CRC32 of seed / clip / second, magnitude 1e-9, identical when the cutoff is fitted and when the decision is made) fixes it and realized fractions then track the targets. Both negative controls PASS: (a) all 7 policies equal admit-all exactly in 168 cells at budget 1.0; (b) replacing each trigger's features with a draw from the fold's pool moves P-trajectory to P-random's level. Consistency check PASS 6/6, check 1 reproducing StudyE9's escalation-32B (both resolvers) and device-only AP to a worst disagreement of 0.000000000000 over 15 comparisons, with one structural difference STATED not reconciled: admit-none's alarm COUNT differs from StudyE9's device-only row because an episode re-arms the up-crossing test at each episode boundary while device-only counts up-crossings over the whole clip; the score stream and therefore AP are identical. Mechanism verification Steps 1-5 recorded before the sweep.** | canonical | PASS (6/6), recorded in report s13. Check 1 reproduces StudyE9's escalation-32B at both resolvers and its device-only baseline to a worst AP disagreement of 0.000000000000 over 15 comparisons; invocations, positives alarmed and false alarms per clip-hour match exactly for admit-all. One structural difference stated: admit-none's alarm count vs StudyE9 device-only (episode-scoped vs whole-clip up-crossings); AP identical. | Steps 1-5 in `results/dashcam/study_e12/e12_mechanism_verification.md`, recorded BEFORE the sweep. | StudyE9b-dense | orchestration | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-11-12, before any computation (user-authorized row). OFFLINE REPLAY OF COMMITTED RESULTS; CPU only on the flash host; no GPU, no model server, no inference. No INVALID result used.** WHY: StudyE9 and StudyE12 replayed latency on StudyD14's 1-s grid with d = ceil(latency), which made the device (0.567 s) and the cloud (about 0.4 s) deliver equally late and put their primary horizon where both models sit near chance. StudyD14b supplies the same 300 clips at a 0.25-s step with integer-t scores bit-identical to D14, and shows most separation lies in the last 0.5 s before the event. METRIC RULES (decided, not varied): Nexar AP at fixed horizons on CONTINUOUS scores (log P(Yes)); no literal Yes/No answer anywhere; alarm, dismiss and trigger are thresholds on continuous scores. Arms are compared WITHIN ONE GRID ONLY (the 0.25-s grid has about 4x the negatives of the 1-s grid, base rate 0.00728 vs 0.0281) and every AP is also reported as a multiple of that grid's chance rate. DELIVERY MODEL (replaces d = ceil(latency) for this study): every score has a compute time t (the decision's frame time) and a delivery time t + L with L the MEASURED latency, not rounded; the application's score at grid time u is the most recent score whose delivery time is <= u (sample-and-hold); AP at horizon h reads the application's score at u = time_of_event - h on the 0.25-s grid, negatives are every grid point of every negative clip. DEVICE (4BI N=1, Jetson Orin): it can start a new score only after the previous one completes, so it scores every ceil(0.567 / 0.25) x 0.25 = 0.75 s, each delivered 0.567 s after its frame time (StudyD8 arm A total p50; the saturated 0.75-s duty is an extrapolation from D8's measured 1 Hz, supported by D8's no-sustained-penalty finding and its back-to-back arm C, and is FLAGGED as such). CLOUD (32BI N=1 and N=3): L = RTT 0.044 s + PNG upload at 13.6 Mbit/s + server TTFT from StudyD7 / D7b at 25 % and 75 % of peak (StudyE9 setting (ii)), with setting (iii) JPEG q90 upload as a LABELLED SENSITIVITY (scores remain the PNG scores; StudyE10a-full did NOT establish S-EMBER JPEG parity, and Nexar ranking parity is cited from StudyE10a, so (iii) stays a sensitivity); resolver cadence c in {0.25, 0.5, 1.0} s with 0.5 s PRIMARY; cloud calls are pipelined (no per-session serialization) and cloud work is charged per resolver call. Setting (i) zero latency on the dense grid is the capability reference. STUDY 1 (E9 rerun, episodes): E9's episode model and procedures UNCHANGED except the grid and the delivery model - trigger on the device's DELIVERED score; resolver calls every c seconds from the trigger; alarm / dismiss on DELIVERED resolver scores; dismiss after k consecutive resolver calls below tau_dismiss (k = 2 primary, 1 and 3 secondary); L_max 10 s of wall clock; cooldown 0; thresholds by 2-fold cross-fit by clip, stratified, seed 0, fitted on the negative grid points of the training fold at per-grid-point FPRs {1, 5, 10 %} and trigger rates {5, 10, 20, 40 %}. POLICIES: device-only; always-cloud (32B at cadence c); escalation-32B; escalation-4B (COUNTERFACTUAL, 4B N=1 resolver at the same cadence c and its own measured cloud latency, so decision rule 1 compares capability at a matched call rate). OUTPUTS as E9: AP at h = 0 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 / 1.0 / 1.5 s, alarm timing, cloud calls and GPU-s per clip-hour, episode outcomes, false dismissals, re-escalations; E9's TWO DECISION RULES UNCHANGED, evaluated at latency setting (ii). Rule 1: capability shortens episodes if escalation-32B has lower total cloud invocations per clip-hour than escalation-4B at matched tau_trig and matched alarm FPR with a paired clip-bootstrap CI excluding 0 in at least 2 of 3 alarm-FPR levels. Rule 2: escalation is efficient if it keeps AP within 0.01 of always-cloud at h <= 1 s with overlapping CIs while using <= 50 % of always-cloud's cloud work at some tau_trig; the 0.01 tolerance is applied UNCHANGED and a scale-matched 0.0026 variant (0.01 x the chance-rate ratio) is reported beside it as a LABELLED SECONDARY, not as the rule. STUDY 2 (E12 rerun, admission): E12's design UNCHANGED (budgets 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75; P-random over 20 seeds, P-score, P-trajectory, P-trajectory-work, P-oracle, admit-all, admit-none; strictly causal features from the device's DELIVERED scores over the last 3 and 5 s plus earlier triggers and earlier episode outcomes and the current cloud latency; NO elapsed-time feature; same utility target, lambda = 150 / 20467 recomputed on this grid's negative grid points with lambda x {0.5, 1, 2} sensitivity; same cross-fitted cutoffs with the CRC32 tiebreak) on the dense grid and delivery model, resolver cadence 0.5 s, setting (ii) at 25 % load primary. E12's DECISION RULE UNCHANGED, evaluated at h = 0.25 s AND at h = 0.5 s, both pre-registered here and both reported. CONTROLS: (a) on integer-t rows only, with d = ceil(latency) and 1-s cadence, this code must reproduce StudyE9's and StudyE12's COMMITTED numbers EXACTLY (end-to-end check; STOP if it fails); (b) zero latency - delivered streams must equal compute streams; (c) E12's own controls - budget 1.0 equals admit-all exactly, and a within-fold feature shuffle collapses P-trajectory to P-random. REPORT: one instrumented episode on the dense grid (scores, delivery times, sample-and-hold values, trigger, resolver calls, outcome) and the CLAUDE.md 6-check consistency protocol. STOP AND ASK IF control (a) fails, any definition cannot be applied as written, or a feature would need information delivered after the decision time. | `experiments/dashcam/study_e9b_dense.py` (engine + Study 1); `experiments/dashcam/study_e9b_admission.py` (Study 2); `experiments/dashcam/study_e9b_report.py` | `results/dashcam/study_d14b/d14b_trace_h100nvl.jsonl.gz` (0.25-s scores, labels, t, time_of_event, duration); `results/dashcam/study_e9/e9_results.json` and `results/dashcam/study_e12/e12_results.json` (reference numbers for control (a)); StudyD4 / D7 / D7b / D8 latency constants via `experiments/dashcam/study_e9_episodes.py` | `results/dashcam/study_e9b/`; `reports/study_e9b_dense.md` | flash (CPU only; no GPU, no model server, no inference) | 300 clips (150 pos / 150 neg), 42,014 grid points per arm (20,467 negative, 21,547 positive); Study 1: 5 latency settings x 3 cadences x 3 resolvers x 3 alarm FPR x 4 trigger rates at the primary episode config, plus k and dismiss-quantile sensitivities; Study 2: 2 resolvers x 3 alarm FPR x 2 trigger rates x 2 latency settings x 4 budgets (incl. 1.0) x 7 policies, P-random 20 seeds; 2-fold cross-fit by clip seed 0; clip bootstrap 2,000 seed 0 stratified by label | **COMPLETE 2026-11-12: THE DELAY DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TIERS IS REAL, AND StudyE9's 1-s GRID HID IT; ON ADMISSION, THE DEVICE SCORE ALONE REMAINS THE SIGNAL.** CONTROL (a) PASSES EXACTLY, which is what licenses everything else: with step 1 s, 1-s cadences and d = ceil(latency) on StudyD14's own trace, this engine reproduces StudyE9's baselines and all 108 primary-config escalation cells and StudyE12's 672 / 672 committed admission cells BIT FOR BIT - worst AP difference 0.000000000000, worst CI difference 0.000000000000, worst scalar relative difference 0.0e+00, zero mismatches over 675 AP+CI and 972 exact-count comparisons in Study 1 plus every cell, diagnostic, AUROC and work-R^2 in Study 2. Variant a2 (StudyD14b's integer rows as input) is ALSO exact (0e+00): the 1-ulp input difference in score = log P(Yes) on 21 of 42,640 rows - flagged in the mechanism verification BEFORE any computation, with p_yes bit-identical on all 42,640 - does not propagate to any threshold comparison or AP ranking. STUDY 1: the link StudyE9 and StudyE12 both carried as CONSTANT - the delay difference between device and cloud, identically zero in every cell of both studies because 0.567 s and 0.395 s both round to 1 s - is instantiated on the 0.25-s grid and is decisive. Under setting (ii) at 25% load the cloud's 32B N=1 answer becomes readable 0.25 s BEFORE the device's own, and the application-visible AP at h = 0 is 0.0384 against the device's 0.0126 (3.0x; 5.3x chance against 1.7x), with the cloud ahead at every horizon reported - where StudyE9's 1-s grid put the same two arms within 0.0005 of each other at h = 0.5 s. The advantage is delay resolution, not call volume: at cadence 1.0 s, StudyE9's own call rate, the cloud still reaches 0.0247 against 0.0126 (2.0x), and raising the cadence to 0.5 s adds only +0.0137 AP for 2.0x the charged cloud work. SECOND HEADLINE, new here: MORE FRAMES IS THE WRONG WAY TO SPEND CAPABILITY ONCE THE PAYLOAD MUST CROSS THE UPLINK. 32B N=3 is the best arm in the study at zero latency (0.0820 at h = 0 against N=1's 0.0451) and falls to 0.0146 delivered - below N=1's 0.0384 - because its 3x PNG payload lifts its hold shift to 1.00 s against N=1's 0.50 s. Server load acts through the hold shift alone: at 75% of peak 32B N=1's shift rises to 0.75 s (equal to the device's) and its AP falls to 0.0196. DECISION RULE 1 reproduces StudyE9's shape - MET at N=1 (40 of 72 cadence x FPR x trigger cells with a CI excluding 0 under (ii)), NOT met at N=3 (8 of 72). DECISION RULE 2 is met in 154 of 360 cells under its UNCHANGED absolute 0.01 AP tolerance but only 40 under the scale-matched 0.0026: on this grid 0.01 AP is 1.37x the entire chance rate, so the wide count is a property of a rule written for a 4x coarser grid, not of the system. THE SCALE-MATCHED COLUMN IS THE ONE TO READ; it was pre-registered before the sweep precisely so the looser count could not be presented as a finding. THIRD HEADLINE, invisible on a 1-s grid by construction: A QUARTER TO TWO FIFTHS OF ALL RESOLVER CALLS ARE ANSWERED AFTER THEIR EPISODE HAS ALREADY CLOSED, and are still charged - at (ii) @25%, cadence 0.5 s, trigger 20%: 32B N=1 25.7%, 32B N=3 39.5%, 4B N=1 22.0% - because with a 1.00 s hold shift against a 0.5 s cadence, 32B N=3 always has two calls in flight when an episode resolves. A direct argument for matching the resolver cadence to the measured latency rather than to the decision grid. FOURTH HEADLINE: ESCALATION ITSELF STAYS WEAK, BECAUSE THE GATE IS THE DEVICE'S OWN STALE SCORE. Device-triggered escalation-32B N=1 captures only 3%/13%/13%/18% of the device-only to always-cloud AP span at h = 0 (trigger 5%/10%/20%/40%) for 11%/21%/36%/61% of always-cloud's calls, and swapping the resolver for the 4B barely moves it: THE BINDING CONSTRAINT ON THIS WORKLOAD IS WHEN TO ASK, NOT WHO ANSWERS. STUDY 2 (StudyE12 re-run, both h = 0.25 s and h = 0.5 s pre-registered): the admission decision rule is NOT MET at neither horizon (best 0 of 3 budgets at h = 0.25 s, best 0 of 3 budgets at h = 0.5 s). Per the pre-registered wording: ON THIS WORKLOAD THE DEVICE SCORE ALONE IS THE ADMISSION SIGNAL. P-score separates from P-random with a CI excluding 0 in 1 of 18 (resolver x horizon x budget) cells in the favourable direction and 1 in the unfavourable one, with the sign changing across budgets within the same resolver and horizon (about 0.9 cells are expected to separate by chance in each direction), so the stronger pre-registered null also fires: ADMISSION RANKING HAS NO SIGNAL ON THIS WORKLOAD as measured by AP. This is a REAL NEGATIVE, not a design failure (CLAUDE.md Rule 2): every link is instantiated non-zero and varying on this grid (Step 2), the Step 4 delivery-link control changes the result, the trajectory predictor separates its training target out of sample (AUROC 0.648-0.764; work model train R^2 0.156-0.187), and the headroom is real - P-oracle reaches 0.0131 at h = 0.25 s / 0.0119 at h = 0.5 s. ALL CONTROLS PASS: (a) end-to-end reproduction exact; (b) zero latency gives delivered = compute for 4 arms x 300 clips; StudyE9's resolver swap exact in 180 cells; CLAUDE.md Step 4 collapses escalation to device-only exactly when L_cloud is raised to L_max; StudyE12's budget-1.0 control exact in 168 cells; the within-fold feature shuffle moves P-trajectory to P-random's level. Consistency check PASS 6/6. ONE CONTROL IS CLASSIFIED HONESTLY RATHER THAN COUNTED AS A PASS: StudyE9's within-clip resolver shuffle is informative under ZERO latency (32B N=3 AP at h = 0 collapses 0.0870 -> 0.0107, 1.5x chance) but is PASS-AS-WRITTEN AND UNINFORMATIVE under measured latency (0.0136 -> 0.0135), because the unshuffled escalation arm is already within 1.9x of chance and the shuffle has nothing left to destroy - the same fact as the trigger-bottleneck headline, seen from the other side. No conclusion rests on the measured cells of that control. THREE LIMITATIONS, all labelled: the device's 0.75 s saturated cadence is extrapolated from StudyD8's measured 1 Hz (the cadence-1.0 s rows are the conservative reading); no cloud capacity limit is modelled (StudyD7 / D7b TTFT at two fixed load points only); and setting (iii) JPEG remains TRANSPORT ONLY - no score was recomputed on JPEG frames and StudyE10a-full did NOT establish S-EMBER parity under its +/-2 pp rule. No AP in this study may be compared with StudyE9's or StudyE12's: chance AP is 0.00728 here against 0.0281 there (3.86x), and every AP is also reported as a multiple of its own grid's chance rate. Mechanism verification Steps 1-5 recorded before the sweep.** | COMPLETE 2026-11-12 | PASS (6/6), recorded in report s14. | StudyE9c-challenge-metric | dashcam | **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-11-12 (later), before any computation (user-authorized row). OFFLINE ANALYSIS OF COMMITTED RESULTS; CPU only on the flash host; no GPU, no model server, no inference. No INVALID result used.** WHY: the project's "Nexar AP" (StudyE5 Part G, StudyE9, StudyE9b) is challenge-STYLE but is NOT the challenge's metric - it scores every decision point of every negative clip as a negative (chance 0.7-2.8 %) and emphasises h = 0 and 0.25 s, whereas the Nexar challenge (arXiv 2503.03848) scores ONE crop per video with roughly balanced classes. This study computes the challenge's metric EXACTLY on our validation clips so that the project's "Nexar metric" claims use Nexar's own definition; the streaming per-decision AP remains reported separately under its own name and NEITHER metric replaces the other. DEFINITIONS (pre-registered, from the paper's evaluation section): CLIPS = the 300 StudyD14b VALIDATION clips (150 pos / 150 neg); NOT the challenge test set, so numbers are NOT leaderboard-comparable. POSITIVES = for each positive clip and horizon h in {0.5, 1.0, 1.5} s, ONE score at the D14b grid point nearest time_of_event - h (the horizon offset is an exact multiple of the 0.25-s step, so horizon separation is exact; StudyE9b's target_index rule, clamped to the clip); a horizon is INCLUDED only if time_of_event - time_of_alert > h for h > 0.5, as the paper does (h = 0.5 always included); counts per horizon reported. NEGATIVES = for each negative clip a fake event time = duration / 2 + Gaussian noise, as the paper does; the paper does NOT state the noise scale, so sigma = the standard deviation of (time_of_event - duration / 2) over the 150 positive clips, seed 0, clipped to [3 s + 1.5 s, duration] so that fake_event - 1.5 s is on the grid; the sigma used is reported; SENSITIVITY: 20 seeds for the fake times, mean and range reported. One stated simplification from the paper: the paper also assigns negatives fake ALERT times to match the positive distribution, which this study does not, so all 150 negatives enter every horizon. METRIC = AP per horizon via scikit-learn `average_precision_score` (NOT the project's streaming `ap_from`, whose tie handling differs; the difference is stated), mAP = UNWEIGHTED mean of the three per-horizon APs; clip bootstrap 2,000, seed 0, stratified by label, shared across arms so every difference is paired; base rate reported per horizon. ARMS AND SETTINGS: ZERO LATENCY - 4BI N1, 32BI N1, 32BI N3, and 4BI N3 (SECONDARY), scores read from StudyD14b at the crop time. DELIVERED - StudyE9b's delivery model exactly (sample-and-hold; device 4BI N1 at a 0.75 s cadence with 0.567 s latency; cloud at cadence 0.5 s with StudyE9b's latency setting (ii) at 25 % and 75 % load), the crop's score being the APPLICATION-VISIBLE score at the crop time; arms = device-only, always-cloud 32BI N1, always-cloud 32BI N3, and StudyE9b's primary escalation-32B N1 at trigger rates 10 % and 20 % using StudyE9b's fold thresholds. REPORT: per arm x setting, AP at 0.5 / 1.0 / 1.5 s and mAP with CIs; paired differences 32B N1 - 4B N1 and always-cloud - device-only; base rate per horizon; a side-by-side table against StudyE9b's streaming AP at the same horizons; and a plain statement of where the two metrics AGREE and where they DIFFER. CONTROLS: (a) zero-latency scores at the crop times must equal StudyD14b's committed trace values EXACTLY; (b) shuffling the labels must give mAP near the base rate. NO CAUSAL CLAIM IS MADE, so CLAUDE.md's mechanism verification Steps 1-5 do not apply (StudyD14b precedent: a metric / input job records its input and control checks before computing instead); the controls above and the definition instantiation are recorded BEFORE any number is produced, and the CLAUDE.md 6-check results consistency protocol is run in full. | `experiments/dashcam/study_e9c_challenge.py`; `experiments/dashcam/study_e9c_report.py` (import `study_e9b_dense.py` for the delivery model, thresholds and episode engine) | `results/dashcam/study_d14b/d14b_trace_h100nvl.jsonl.gz` (0.25-s scores, labels, t, time_of_event, time_of_alert, duration); `results/dashcam/study_e9b/e9b_study1_results.json` (streaming AP for the side-by-side); StudyD4 / D7 / D7b / D8 latency constants via `experiments/dashcam/study_e9_episodes.py`; Nexar challenge paper arXiv 2503.03848 (evaluation section) for the metric definition | `results/dashcam/study_e9c/`; `reports/study_e9c_challenge_metric.md` | flash (CPU only; no GPU, no model server, no inference) | 300 clips (150 pos / 150 neg); 3 horizons; 4 zero-latency arms + 5 delivered arms x 2 load settings; 20 fake-time seeds for the negative-crop sensitivity; clip bootstrap 2,000 seed 0 stratified by label | **COMPLETE 2026-11-12 (later): THE PROJECT'S "NEXAR AP" WAS MEASURING THE RIGHT ORDERING ON THE WRONG SCALE. EVERY SIGN AND ORDERING SURVIVES TRANSLATION INTO NEXAR'S OWN METRIC; ONE COMMITTED FINDING IS NARROWED IN SCOPE.** The Nexar challenge's evaluation (arXiv 2503.03848) was reproduced exactly on our 300 validation clips: ONE crop per clip, horizons 0.5 / 1.0 / 1.5 s, a horizon included only when time_of_event - time_of_alert > h (h = 0.5 always), negatives cropped at duration/2 + Gaussian noise, scikit-learn AP per horizon, unweighted mAP. Positive crops per horizon 150 / 106 / 66 against 150 negatives, so CHANCE AP IS 0.500 / 0.414 / 0.306 (mean 0.407) against the streaming metric's 0.00728 - a factor of 56, which is the whole point. sigma for the fake event times is NOT given by the paper; pre-registered as the sample s.d. of (time_of_event - duration/2) over the positives = 2.8905 s, and every headline is recomputed over 20 seeds (mAP spread up to 0.072, ordering stable in all of them). RESULTS. Zero latency mAP: 4B N1 0.5223, 32B N1 0.6368, 32B N3 0.6493, 4B N3 (secondary) 0.5332. Delivered under StudyE9b's model at (ii) @25 %: device-only 0.4776, always-cloud 32B N1 0.5675, 32B N3 0.5504, escalation-32B trig10 0.4591, trig20 0.4855; at @75 % the cloud falls to 0.5508. WHAT AGREES WITH THE STREAMING METRIC (same sign, CI excluding 0 under both): 32B beats 4B at zero latency (challenge mAP +0.1144 [+0.0611, +0.1686]); the delivered cloud beats the delivered device (+0.0899 [+0.0357, +0.1497] at 25 % load and +0.0732 [+0.0179, +0.1296] at 75 %); server load costs the cloud part of its advantage; and ESCALATION BUYS NOTHING at these horizons (-0.0185 at trigger 10 %, +0.0079 at 20 %, neither CI excluding 0), which is StudyE9b's finding 7 from the other side. WHAT DOES NOT TRANSFER. (1) LEVELS never transfer. (2) EXCESS-OVER-CHANCE RATIOS transfer to within 35 % for the three delivered contrasts, where both arms sit near chance, but COMPRESS BY A FACTOR OF TWO for the zero-latency 32B-vs-4B contrast (1.63x here against 3.26x streaming), because with 150 balanced negatives 4B N1 already reaches 0.673 at h = 0.5 s and the balanced metric is near its ceiling. THE USABLE RULE: signs and orderings transfer unconditionally; excess-over-chance ratios transfer only while both arms are near chance; absolute AP levels never transfer. (3) ONE COMMITTED FINDING IS NARROWED: StudyE9b's finding 2, "32B N = 3 falls BELOW N = 1 once delivered", is a statement about h = 0 and h = 0.25 s. N = 3 retains 23% of N = 1's excess over chance at h = 0 but 89% on the challenge's mAP, and N = 3 still beats device-only with a CI excluding 0 (+0.0727 [+0.0035, +0.1362]). StudyE9b's finding is NOT WRONG - it was computed at the horizons the streaming metric emphasises - but it must be quoted as holding for a system that acts at h <= about 0.25 s. A PROPERTY OF THE CHALLENGE'S OWN RULE, reported because it affects reading: the alert rule drops 44 of 150 positives at h = 1.0 s and 84 at h = 1.5 s, so the longer horizons are scored on a self-selected, easier subset against the full negative set, and chance itself falls from 0.500 to 0.306. CONTROLS BOTH PASS: (a) all 3,088 zero-latency crops equal StudyD14b's committed trace values exactly, with every positive crop within half a grid step of its target (worst 0.1240 s against 0.125 s) and 0 crops clamped; (b) label shuffling drives mAP to 1.02-1.03x the base rate. Consistency check PASS 6/6. TERMINOLOGY DECISION: "Nexar AP" now has two precise meanings and must always be qualified - CHALLENGE mAP (this study) or STREAMING AP (StudyE5 Part G / E9 / E9b). NEITHER REPLACES THE OTHER: the streaming metric remains the right instrument for a standing query that must decide at every tick, and it is the only one that speaks to h < 0.5 s, which the challenge does not score. SCOPE: validation clips, not the challenge test set, so NOT leaderboard-comparable; and the input is a single decision from a 3-s frame window, not the challenge's ~10 s crop - the evaluation GEOMETRY is reproduced exactly, the input FORMAT is not. No causal claim is made, so CLAUDE.md Steps 1-5 do not apply (StudyD14b precedent); the controls and definitions were recorded before any number was produced.** | COMPLETE 2026-11-12 | PASS (6/6), recorded in report s10.
